# Bootstrap ledger

Until `factory approve` exists (F-04), nothing can legally write `.factory/tasks.json`, so its statuses stay `draft`. Progress during the bootstrap is recorded **here** instead, one entry per work order, with evidence.

F-04 AC-4 reconciles this ledger into `tasks.json`: each entry is approved and set to its final status through the normal state machine. After that, this file is frozen.

Entries record evidence, not claims. Each criterion is marked proven, pending, or not met.

---

## F-00 — Phase 0 owner prerequisites

- **Kind:** `owner_only` (no acceptance criteria; six owner steps). Recorded 2026-10-16.
- **Step 1, Z.ai support email** (unattended Agent SDK runs on the Coding Plan; legacy Max renewal and quota): owner-confirmed, 2026-10-16.
- **Step 2, legacy term end date** checked on Z.ai's "My plan" page: owner-confirmed, 2026-10-16.
- **Step 3, JobFinderOS production `GLM_BASE_URL`** not on the Coding Plan endpoint before F-13: owner-confirmed, 2026-10-16.
- **Step 4, JobFinderOS `main` branch protection:** verified 2026-10-16 by reading `GET repos/AgenticTony/JobFinderOS/branches/main/protection`. PRs are required (0 approvals; solo owner can't approve their own PRs). 7 status checks are required, all pinned to GitHub Actions (`app_id` 15368), with strict up-to-date. `enforce_admins` is on, so the admin token the factory pushes with can't bypass. Force-push and deletion are blocked. The protection was absent before this date (the classic API returned 404, and there were no rulesets); the owner applied it.
- **Step 5, keychain items `JFO_PROBE_PASS` / `JFO_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`:** owner-confirmed, 2026-10-16. Not inspected; the factory doesn't read the keychain outside a run.
- **Step 6, private GitHub repo for the factory:** verified 2026-10-16. `gh repo view AgenticTony/software-factory-agents` reports `PRIVATE`.
- **Why this entry has no reconcile status:** `factory approve` refuses `owner_only` kinds, and no command yet ticks owner steps in `tasks.json`. A reconcile status marker (the bold line other entries end with) would make every reconcile run fail on F-00, so this section is deliberately prose the parser skips. The gap is that F-00 can't reach `done` through the state machine, and F-13 depends on it. That needs a mechanism (an owner-step tick command, or reconcile support for owner-only entries) before F-13.

## F-03 — State machine and picker

- **Commits:** `feat/f03-state-machine` → [PR #6](https://github.com/AgenticTony/software-factory-agents/pull/6). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.state` module before implementation. First push drew 10 review findings (2 reproduced: concurrent writers silently losing updates; `ensure_ascii` escaping every non-ASCII character); all 10 fixed on the same PR, see the review-round notes below.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session, then a review round the same day.
- **Design notes:** TASK-CONTRACT §3 as an explicit 23-edge `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` table; the eight `X -> draft` edges exist solely for "contract changed" (§2), with `demote_changed_contracts()` as their caller — a demoted task needs human re-approval (F-04), so it can never auto-retry. `not_buildable` has no outgoing edges (importer sets it at creation). The §4 picker is a pure function plus a `TaskQueue` store that is the only write path: validate (F-02 `validate_queue`, new public fn in contract.py) → append-only attempts check → temp-file+`os.replace` atomic write → roll back memory on any failure.
- **Review-round changes (all 10 findings):**
  - **Concurrent writers** (reproduced): `save()` now takes an exclusive `flock` on `tasks.json.lock` (never renamed, so it's a stable lock point), re-reads the queue under it, and refuses with `ConcurrentWriteError` if the file changed since load — `factory loop` and a human `factory approve` can no longer lose each other's writes. The append-only check now runs against the queue re-read from disk, not the stale snapshot.
  - **Slice continuation:** `deploying → ready` added; guards enforce that `done`/`awaiting_owner` require every slice merged and continuation requires an unmerged one. TASK-CONTRACT §3 gained a recovery/continuation edge table.
  - **Stale `in_progress`:** `in_progress → ready` (aborted run) edge + `reclaim_abandoned(now)` using last-attempt `started` + `budget.wall_clock_min`.
  - **Reclaim follow-up (2nd review):** the timestamp rule could reclaim a *live* run (setup and validation sit outside the session clock) and missed a crash before the first attempt record. Replaced with a **run lease**: an OS `flock` taken by `begin_run()` before the `ready → in_progress` transition and held for the session's life. `reclaim_abandoned()` acts only when the lease is free. Proven by `test_reclaim_never_takes_a_live_run`, `test_reclaim_frees_a_crash_before_the_first_attempt`, `test_begin_run_refuses_a_second_concurrent_run`, and `test_lease_is_freed_when_the_holding_process_dies` (a real subprocess exits via `os._exit` while holding the lease).
  - **`done` on criteria change:** decision recorded — `done` tasks are never auto-demoted; `demote_changed_contracts` returns them in `flagged_done` for owner review. Reopening (`done → draft`) stays a human action. TASK-CONTRACT §2 updated.
  - **`ensure_ascii=False`** (reproduced): the queue keeps literal em-dashes/arrows; no escape-noise diffs.
  - **Duplicate task ids** now rejected in `validate_queue` with a readable error.
  - **`StateError`/`TransitionError`/`ConcurrentWriteError` subclass `ContractError`** so `except ContractError` handlers catch state failures too.
  - **PR rejection:** `in_review → in_progress` (fix round) edge.
  - **Dependency drift:** `ready → blocked` edge + `sync_readiness()` reconciling both directions; cross-repo deps keep a task blocked.
  - **Picker O(n²):** the id index is built once per `pick_next` call.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f03.py::test_ac_1` walks **all** 100 status pairs — the 23 table edges return their reason, the other 77 raise `TransitionError`; the store-level refusal leaves file and memory untouched. One test per illegal edge class: `test_illegal_edge_class_raises` (self / forward skip / backward / lateral / exit-from-needs_human / exit-from-not_buildable).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — any `in_progress` task makes `pick_next` and `TaskQueue.pick` return None.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — P1 later in the queue beats P2 earlier; equal priority resolved by queue order; draft/blocked/in_review and decision/research/owner_only skipped; `docs` picked.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a `repo:id` dep is never met, even when a task with that literal id sits `done` in the same queue; same-repo dep on a `done` task is met (`test_dependency_must_be_done_in_this_queue` covers open and missing deps).
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — external acceptance edit without a hash update → `demote_changed_contracts()` demotes to `draft` and persists; a matching hash leaves the task untouched; `test_done_task_with_changed_contract_is_flagged_not_demoted` covers the `done` carve-out.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — `os.replace` crashing mid-write leaves the previous `tasks.json` byte-identical, loadable, and no temp junk behind; `test_crash_during_serialisation_leaves_file_intact` covers the earlier crash window.
- **Extra invariants tested:** append-only `attempts` (rewrite, clear, and task-removal each refused with file intact), `save` refuses schema-invalid state, attempts are schema-validated on append, `updated_at` stamped per write, slice selection (first incomplete / fall-through fully merged / unsliced = one unit), concurrency (both a lost-append and a lost-transition scenario), non-ASCII round-trip, duplicate-id rejection, error hierarchy, new edges, `reclaim_abandoned`, `sync_readiness`.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-04 — `factory approve`

- **Commits:** `feat/f04-approve` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.approve` module before implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session, then this ledger entry.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/approve.py` — `approve_task` (render contract → checks → confirm → freeze `acceptance_hash`/`approved_at`/`approved_by` → `draft -> ready|blocked` per the picker's dependency rule) and `reconcile_bootstrap` (parse BOOTSTRAP.md, per interactive entry approve + validate the legal F-03 path to the recorded status, written in one save — see review round). Wired into `factory/cli.py` as a real subcommand (`--yes`, `--reconcile-bootstrap`, `--queue`, `--ledger`, `--approved-by`; refusals exit 1, never a traceback). `factory/state.py` gained public `unmet_dependencies` (the §4 rule, named for reuse) and `now_iso` (renamed from `_now_iso` so approval stamps timestamps in the queue's style). `gates.spec_approval` is **not** touched by approval: every task in this repo's queue already carries `true` at draft, so it reads as "this gate applies", not "approved" — the frozen-contract markers are the three fields above.
  - Reconcile rules beyond the literal WO text, recorded for review: a criterion the ledger never mentions also blocks `done` (evidence per criterion is what the ledger exists to record); entries whose dependency isn't reconciled yet fail with the dep named (reconcile runs in file = dependency order); already-reconciled entries are skipped, so a re-run after a partial reconcile is safe; a declined entry is skipped, and its dependents then fail on the open dependency.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f04.py::test_ac_1` — empty `acceptance` refused with a readable error, file byte-identical.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — dep-free → `ready`; open dep → `blocked`; done dep → `ready`; hash = the F-02 formula, `approved_at`/`approved_by` stamped. Cross-repo dep stays `blocked` (extra test).
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — `--yes` on a risk-flagged task refused before anything is printed or written; interactive confirmation approves it.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — fixture ledger: F-1/F-2 reconciled to `done` through legal transitions in dependency order, one interactive confirmation per entry that gets that far; the pending entry is refused *before* asking (no point confirming a refusal), stays `draft`, no hash. Extra tests: unmentioned criterion also blocks `done`; declined entry skipped with dependents failing on the dep; second run skips; intermediate target (`in_review`) walks the full path; risk-flagged entry confirms interactively; the parser and a full reconcile run against **this repo's real** BOOTSTRAP.md + tasks.json (F-01..F-03 → done, F-04 untouched).
- **Extra coverage:** non-draft and non-executable-kind refusals, declined confirmation writes nothing, contract rendering includes ids/texts/checks, CLI end-to-end (`--yes` exit 0, risk `--yes` exit 1, `--reconcile-bootstrap`, task-id/reconcile exclusivity).
- **Smoke-tested by hand** on a copy of the real queue: `approve F-04 --yes` → `blocked` (F-03 still draft); interactive `y` on F-11 → `blocked`; `approve F-05 --yes` (risk) → refused, exit 1; `--reconcile-bootstrap` with three `y`s → F-01..F-03 `done`.
- **Review round (10 findings, all fixed on this PR; 3 reproduced):**
  - **Stranding + lease bypass + save churn (reproduced, one structural fix):** reconcile no longer persists approval and each walk step as separate saves — a failure partway left an approved task stranded at an intermediate status that a re-run then skipped, and the transient `in_progress` never held the run lease (F-03's `reclaim_abandoned` could grab it mid-walk). New `TaskQueue.transition_path(statuses)`: validates every consecutive edge + final-status guards, applies only the final status in **one** atomic save. Proven by `test_reconcile_failure_writes_nothing` (the reviewer's unmerged-slice repro: entry fails, file byte-identical, re-run re-asks) and `test_reconcile_writes_once_per_entry` (2 entries → 2 saves).
  - **`done` with open owner steps (reproduced):** F-03 gained an owner-step guard — `done` is refused while any `owner_step` is open, and `awaiting_owner` is refused when none are (it would wait on nothing). Reconcile routes a recorded `done` with open steps to `awaiting_owner`, with the reroute named in the result detail. `test_l4_branches_from_deploying` now ticks the step before `done` (the edge was legal before; the semantics weren't enforced). Real case: F-06 can't close over its Z.ai owner step.
  - **EOF crash (reproduced):** `input()` at EOF now declines instead of raising `EOFError` past the CLI's error handling; the CLI catches `KeyboardInterrupt` → exit 130. `factory approve X < /dev/null` exits 1 with "approval declined".
  - **`--reconcile-bootstrap --yes` (defeated AC-4's per-entry confirmation):** now a usage error at the parser — reconcile is always interactive, every entry, because the ledger is plain markdown this command turns into `done` tasks.
  - **Pending criteria beyond `done`:** the all-proven requirement now covers every recorded status that asserts the build passed validation (`in_review`, `deploying`, `awaiting_owner`, `done`); `needs_human` stays exempt (escalation is where unproven criteria honestly land).
  - **Parser hardening:** heading ids stripped of trailing punctuation (`## F-05: …` → `F-05`), and a status marker that fails to parse (backticks missing, say) still counts as an entry with `status=None`, so reconcile fails it visibly instead of silently dropping a work order.
  - **Cleanups:** unreachable post-approval dependency check deleted (subsumed by the pre-check once the save is atomic); dead `dependencies_met` removed (one public spelling of the rule now: `unmet_dependencies`).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-07 — L0 deterministic gates

- **Commits:** `feat/f07-l0-gates` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.l0` module before implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session, then a self-review round (3 findings, all fixed on this PR; see below).
- **Design notes:** new `factory/l0.py` — `run_l0(profile, repo, base, head, task, baseline)` computes `git diff -M base...head`, applies the diff policy (`check_diff_policy`), then runs the profile commands in gate order (setup → lint → typecheck → test → build → sorted extras) and compares output against the baseline. Baseline file format (repo-relative path from `profile.baselines`): `{"known_errors": {file: [lines]}, "min_tests": N}` — the exact `file:line` precision rule and the test-count floor ported from SoloAdventurer's `verifier.md`/`baselines.json` (whole-file exemptions never allowed). Protected set = `paths.protected` globs **plus the task's contract test files** (locked at approval). Escalations (`new_dependency`, `risk_path_touched`, `protected_path_touched`) are immediate-stop classes recorded separately from FAIL findings; risk flags found in the diff come back as `risk_flags_added` for F-10 to write onto the task. A failing command with no parsable `file:line` location FAILs with the raw output (an unattributable error is not pre-existing). The `new_dependency` registry checks (package exists / >30 days / near-miss name) are deferred: they need the F-05 sandbox network allowlist; the escalation itself is recorded now.
  - Self-review findings fixed on this PR: (1) `+++ /dev/null` in deleted-file diffs was counted as an added line — unified-diff parsing now only tracks content inside `@@` hunks; (2) `T` (typechange) status escaped the protected-path check — now treated as a modification (`C` copies as additions); (3) a malformed baseline (`known_errors` not a dict, non-numeric `min_tests`) crashed or silently weakened the gate — now a `baseline_invalid` FAIL with no exemptions applied.
  - `/code-review` findings fixed on this PR (top 5 of 10; each with a red-first test in `tests/test_f07.py`): (1) a timed-out command that had printed to stdout crashed `run_l0` (POSIX `TimeoutExpired` carries raw bytes + `None`) — output is now normalised, and undecodable output is replaced, not raised (`test_timeout_with_partial_stdout_does_not_crash`); (2) `TEST_GLOBS` were root-anchored, so `backend/tests/…` (the pilot's layout) and colocated `pkg/test_*.py` escaped all three test rules — nested globs added (`test_nested_test_dir_is_recognised`); (3) context lines didn't advance the hunk counters, so finding line numbers were wrong — fixed, and `git diff` now pins `--no-color --no-ext-diff` and `a/`/`b/` prefixes so user git config can't blind the parser (`test_diff_line_numbers_count_context_lines`); (4) the baseline was read from the slice's own checkout, so a worker could widen `known_errors` or lower `min_tests` — it is now read with `git show <base>:<path>` (`test_baseline_is_read_from_base_not_head`); (5) a failed command whose parsed locations were all baselined passed even when it also reported an unattributable failure — `FAILURE_MARKERS` (pytest `FAILED`/`ERROR`, `N failed`, TAP `not ok`, tracebacks, tool-level `error:`) now FAIL it; baseline exemptions cover lint/typecheck diagnostics only, per `verifier.md`'s precision rule (`test_baselined_location_cannot_mask_unattributed_failure`). The remaining 5 were deferred to a follow-up PR (next bullet).
  - Deferred `/code-review` findings, fixed in a follow-up PR on `fix/f07-l0-deferred-findings` (each red-first in `tests/test_f07.py`): (6) pilot commands run `cd backend && …`, so reported paths were backend-relative while the baseline is repo-relative — a single unquoted leading `cd <dir> &&` now sets the base parsed paths are joined onto, then normalised (strips `./`; absolute in-repo paths made relative); anything else is left as reported, so it matches no baseline entry and FAILs (`test_cwd_relative_error_paths_match_repo_baseline`); (7) SoloAdventurer's real `.claude/state/baselines.json` uses `known_typecheck_errors.files` + `jest.tests`, which L0 silently ignored — both schemas are now read (error lists merge, the higher floor wins; checked against the live file: floor 464, no exemptions), a baseline with none of the recognised sections is `baseline_invalid`, and a declared floor with no readable `N passed` count is the new FAIL `test_count_unknown` instead of a silent skip (`test_soloadventurer_baseline_schema_is_supported`, `test_baseline_without_recognised_keys_fails`, `test_unreadable_test_count_does_not_skip_floor`); a baseline with error lists but no floor is allowed and leaves a note; (8) non-int line entries crashed `int(line)` and `min_tests: true`/floats passed the type check — both are now `baseline_invalid` with no exemptions (`test_bad_baseline_entries_are_invalid`, 7 cases); (9) `run_l0` now raises `GateError` unless `HEAD` resolves to `head_ref`'s commit and `git status --porcelain` is empty (`test_checkout_not_at_head_ref_raises`, `test_dirty_working_tree_raises`); (10) renaming a test file to a non-test path (pure R100, no hunks) is now `test_deleted` on the old path (`test_rename_test_to_non_test_path_is_deleted`). Behaviour change: a declared baseline file containing `{}` is now `baseline_invalid` (was: nothing exempted, no floor).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f07.py::test_ac_1` — modified `schema/**` file → verdict `fail`, escalation + finding `protected_path_touched` naming the file. Extra: deleted and renamed-away protected files also FAIL (`test_deleted_protected_file_fails`, `test_rename_out_of_protected_path_fails`), and a contract test file (`tests/test_thing.py` from the acceptance ref) is protected even when not in `paths.protected` (`test_contract_test_file_is_protected`).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — modified `pyproject.toml` → escalation `new_dependency`, verdict `pass` (escalation ≠ FAIL), no findings.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — modified `src/pay/**` file with `task.risk=[]` → `risk_flags_added == ["payments"]`, escalation `risk_path_touched`, verdict `pass`. Extra: with the flag declared, no escalation and nothing added (`test_declared_risk_flag_does_not_escalate`).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a deleted `tests/test_alpha.py` → FAIL `test_deleted`; an added `@pytest.mark.skip` line → FAIL `skip_marker_added`. Extra: JS `it.only(`/`xit(` markers FAIL (`test_js_only_and_pending_markers_fail`), removed assertions FAIL (`test_removed_assertion_fails`).
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — a failing lint whose output carries `lib/foo.py:10` (baselined) and `lib/foo.py:20` (new): verdict `fail` with exactly one `new_error` finding at `lib/foo.py:20`, line 10 noted pre-existing; the same command reporting only the baselined line 10 → verdict `pass` despite exit 1. Extra: tsc-style `src/x.ts(12,5): error TS…` parsed (`test_tsc_style_errors_are_parsed`); unparsable failing output → FAIL `command_failed`; `min_tests` floor enforced (`test_test_count_floor`), and SoloAdventurer's `known_typecheck_errors.files`/`jest.tests` keys honoured (`test_soloadventurer_baseline_schema_is_supported`).
- **Extra coverage:** setup failure skips remaining gates; command timeout (`command_timeout`); missing baseline file (`baseline_missing`); malformed baseline structure (`baseline_invalid`, no exemptions); clean diff + green commands → pass with no findings/escalations; pure-function units (`contract_test_files`, `parse_errors`, `parse_test_count`, `diff_file_changes` incl. rename line-tracking).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-08 — L1 red-first proof

- **Commits:** `feat/f08-l1-red-first` → [PR #9](https://github.com/AgenticTony/software-factory-agents/pull/9), merged into `feat/f07-l0-gates` — **but the merge captured the pre-review-fix head `f7f0c81`**: the review fixes were force-pushed as `f7a432a` moments too late. They are re-applied here (cherry-picked onto the branch tip; the conflict with PR #10's concurrent L0 fixes resolved so #10's new helpers stay in `l0.py` while the shared machinery still moves to `gates.py`). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.l1` module before implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session, then a review round (10 findings, all addressed below).
- **Design notes:** new `factory/l1.py` — `run_l1(profile, repo, base, head, task)` proves every `check.type: test` ref in the approved contract red-first: force-checkout `head` → existence pre-check per ref (`path::name` shape via shared `ref_path`, file present on head) → **red** (`git checkout --detach` at the **merge-base** of base and head — the tree `base...head` was computed against, never base's tip — apply ONLY the diff's test-file changes with each *path* judged independently (A/C/M/T test files checked out from head, D removed, a rename's two halves each decided by its own side), then run each ref; exit 0 = `test_proves_nothing`; collection errors count as red, the question being "would it fail without the change") → **green** (`git checkout --force <head>`, run each ref; non-zero = `test_not_green`, or `contract_ambiguous` when the runner's own not-found lines appear — anchored `^ERROR: not found:` / `^no tests ran in`, so failing output containing "404 Not Found" stays a worker problem). `git clean -fdx` runs at phase boundaries: a red-phase `__pycache__` survives a same-size checkout (Python's mtime+size check) and would make green run base's bytecode. The per-ref command comes from a new optional `commands.test_ref` shell template with `{ref}`/`{path}`/`{name}`/`{python}` placeholders (path/name separate for jest-style runners), substituted by **literal replacement** — `str.format` semantics made `${VAR}` a KeyError; default `python -m pytest -q {ref}` (`python -m` puts the repo root on `sys.path` the way a project's own CI does); `schema/project.schema.json` gained the optional key. The repo is always left on `head_ref` (try/finally force checkout + clean; a restore failure never masks the gate's error). Shared machinery now lives once in **new `factory/gates.py`** (GateError, Finding, CommandResult, FileChange, GateReport base, TEST_GLOBS, git/run_command, `diff_file_changes` + new name-status-only `diff_name_status`, `contract_test_refs`/`contract_test_files`/`ref_path`); `factory.l0` imports from it (re-exporting what it uses) and `factory.l1` builds on it — no more cross-module private imports or duplicated report serialisation. Deferred: mapping the non-test acceptance items to evidence (command exit codes, scheduled holdout/probe checks) — that report assembly belongs to F-10.
- **Review round (10 findings; 9 fixed as suggested, 1 fixed differently with cause, all on this PR):**
  - **Red at base tip vs merge-base** (reproduced): red now checks out `git merge-base base head`. Proven by `test_main_fixing_impl_after_branch_does_not_fake_red` (main fixed impl post-branch → old code gave a false `test_proves_nothing`) and `test_parallel_deletion_on_main_does_not_crash_the_red_phase` (main deleted the same file → old code's `git rm` raised `GateError`).
  - **Stale `__pycache__` between phases**: `git clean -fdx` at every phase boundary. Proven by `test_red_bytecode_cannot_leak_into_green` (same-size edit, the exact mtime+size hazard, plus a no-leftovers assertion).
  - **`_NOT_FOUND` matched failure output**: anchored on pytest's own lines (`^ERROR: not found:`, `^no tests ran in`). Proven by `test_not_found_text_in_failure_output_is_not_ambiguous`.
  - **Mixed renames leaked/moved implementation files in red**: per-path application instead of per-change. Proven by `test_rename_out_of_tests_dir_does_not_leak_impl_into_red` (old code produced a false `test_proves_nothing`) and `test_rename_into_tests_dir_keeps_base_implementation`.
  - **Collection errors counted as red** — fixed differently than suggested: the reviewer's exit-code split (reject 2/4/5 as red) is empirically wrong — a missing fixture is **exit 1** and a not-yet-written import inside a test is also **exit 1**/legitimately red (verified against pytest 8), so the split would both miss the scenario and break canonical red-first. The actual hole — a diff-shipped `conftest.py` masking a proves-nothing test — is closed by treating `conftest.py` at any depth as a test file (`gates.TEST_GLOBS`), so red applies it. Proven by `test_diff_conftest_applies_in_red_phase`. Residual (documented in the module docstring): a non-glob helper shipped by the diff and imported by the test can still mask a red; the red output rides in the report for the L2 reviewer.
  - **Full unified diff parsed unused**: new `gates.diff_name_status` (name-status only); L1 uses it, L0 keeps `diff_file_changes`.
  - **`contract_test_refs` duplicated the acceptance walk**: one canonical pair in `gates` — `contract_test_refs` + `contract_test_files` derived from it via shared `ref_path` (strip bug gone; unit-tested).
  - **Report/private-helper duplication**: shared `factory/gates.py` extraction described above; `L0Report`/`L1Report` subclass `GateReport` (serialisation in one place); F-07's suite passes unchanged against the extraction.
  - **Template couldn't serve jest; `${VAR}` → KeyError**: `{path}`/`{name}` placeholders added and substitution switched to literal replacement (schema description updated). Proven by `test_template_path_and_name_placeholders` and `test_template_shell_braces_pass_through`; the old broken-template `GateError` test was removed with `str.format`.
  - **No merge-base/stale-bytecode fixtures**: the tests above close exactly that gap.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f08.py::test_ac_1` — the named test passes on base with only the diff's test files applied → verdict `fail`, finding `test_proves_nothing`; the red run's exit 0 is asserted, so the reported FAIL *is* the proves-nothing case.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — proper fixture (base fails the test; the diff adds the test and the fix) → verdict `pass`, red exit != 0, green exit == 0, and `to_dict()` carries the L1 report shape F-09/F-10 consume.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — named file absent from the branch → `contract_ambiguous` before any run (no command results); file present but the named test absent → `contract_ambiguous` at green via the runner's not-found output, not `test_not_green`.
- **Extra coverage:** a ref without `::` → ambiguous, nothing runs; honest red + a diff that doesn't fix the code → `test_not_green`; test committed before its impl (diff touches only code) → valid red-first PASS; command timeout → `command_timeout`; custom `test_ref` template used verbatim; path/name placeholders; shell braces pass through; a contract with no test checks → pass (nothing to prove); deleted and test-to-test-renamed files apply in the red phase without crashing; the repo is left on the slice branch, clean, after both PASS and FAIL runs; `contract_test_refs` order/dedup and `ref_path` strip units; `load_profile` accepts `commands.test_ref`.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-05 — Clone manager and sandbox

- **Commits:** `feat/f05-clone-sandbox` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.runs` module before implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session, then this ledger entry.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/runs.py` (clone manager) + `factory/sandbox.py` (srt integration), built to the owner's mid-session direction: **srt itself, not a hand-rolled Seatbelt wrapper**.
  - **srt pinned three ways:** `package.json` pins `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime` at exactly `"0.0.79"` (no `^`/`~`), the committed `package-lock.json` freezes the install (CI and the Mac both run `npm ci` from it), and `ensure_srt()` runs `srt --version` before anything is sandboxed — any value other than `0.0.79` refuses with the fix in the message. `run_sandboxed()` calls it on every spawn; F-06/F-10 will also call it at session/loop start. `FACTORY_SRT_BIN` overrides the binary path for tests (fakes must still report the pin or they're refused).
  - **Settings from the profile** (`build_settings`, written by `write_settings` next to the runs root under `<factory_root>/sandbox/`): `filesystem.allowWrite = [run_dir]` only; `denyRead` = holdouts root, `~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh` and the **whole runs root** (stricter than "other projects": sibling runs of the same project are denied too), with `allowRead = [run_dir]` re-allowing the run's own tree (allowRead beats denyRead per srt semantics); `network.allowedDomains` = model endpoint hostname (URL or bare host accepted) + `docs_allowlist` + `network_allowlist`, `deniedDomains: []` (srt requires the key and refuses invalid files rather than falling back to defaults — verified empirically).
  - **Env, two layers** (AC-4): `sandbox_env()` builds a minimal allowlist env (PATH/HOME/LANG/TZ + run-dir TMPDIR + scrubbed extras) so an interactive orchestrator shell can't leak into a session; `scrub_env()` drops the structural GitHub token names, every `GH_*`/`GITHUB_*` variable, and caller-named keychain-derived names (the Z.ai model token is deliberately not on any list — it is the one secret the sandbox holds). Backstop: the settings file denies the same names via `credentials.envVars` (`{"mode": "deny", "name": ...}`), enforced by srt even when an unscrubbed env is handed straight to it — proven by invoking srt directly with a leaky env in `test_ac_4`.
  - **TMPDIR points inside the run dir** (`<run_dir>/.tmp`): writes are allowed nowhere else, and tools that need scratch (pip builds, the macOS python xcrun shim) would otherwise break.
  - **Command transport:** `srt -c <shlex.join(cmd)>`. The variadic form joins argv naively and mangles quoting (reproduced); `-c` takes one command string verbatim, so argv survives the shell round trip exactly.
  - **Clone manager:** `create_run()` runs the WO's exact command (`git clone --filter=blob:none` from the local repo via `file://` URI), optionally detach-checkouts a ref — resolved to a commit via `ref` → `origin/<ref>` first, because a branch that exists only as a remote ref sends `checkout --detach` down git's DWIM branch-creation path, which rejects `--detach` (reproduced on git 2.50) — seeds a warm dependency cache (`shutil.copytree` into the run dir), and returns the `RunDir`. `cleanup_run()` removes clone + settings file and is idempotent. Run ids: `<UTC stamp>-<6 hex>`, created under `runs/<project>/`.
  - **Known local-git behaviour, recorded:** on this Mac's git 2.50 (Homebrew), the local `file://` server ignores the blob filter ("filtering not recognized by server, ignoring") while git still records `remote.origin.promisor=true` + `partialclonefilter=blob:none`. The command is exactly the WO's; a fully-materialised clone is still correct, just not lazy.
  - **CI (owner decision, up front):** sandbox tests RUN on Ubuntu, never skipped. The workflow installs `bubblewrap socat ripgrep`, lifts Ubuntu 24.04's AppArmor unprivileged-userns block (`sudo sysctl -w kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=0`) before pytest, and installs srt from the committed lockfile (`npm ci` + setup-node 22). There is no skip mechanism anywhere: locally a missing srt fails `ensure_srt` (and the tests) with the fix in the message; on CI a broken stack is a red run. `test_ac_3` makes a live, unauthenticated HTTPS request to `api.z.ai` from inside the sandbox (connection success only, no credentials); a Z.ai outage is a labelled CI failure, not a sandbox bug — the assertion message says so and the example.com control in the same test distinguishes the two.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f05.py::test_ac_1` — inside a real srt sandbox: write inside the run dir succeeds (and reads back); writes to the factory root outside the run dir and to `/tmp` raise EPERM; the target files don't exist afterwards.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — a real read attempt on `<factory_root>/holdouts/scenario.md` raises EPERM, while a control read inside the run dir succeeds (the denial is targeted, not a blanket read-block).
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — from inside the sandbox, curl to `https://api.z.ai` returns a real HTTP status (any 1xx–5xx proves the unauthenticated connection; "000" would mean no response) while curl to non-allowlisted `https://example.com` fails at the srt proxy (CONNECT 403 → http_code 000). srt's network model is proxy-based: the child gets authenticated proxy env vars and allowlist enforcement happens at the proxy; direct socket DNS inside the sandbox is denied by design.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a child env carrying `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_HOST` and a keychain-derived name shows all of them absent inside the sandbox while a permitted extra (`API_TIMEOUT_MS`) survives; plus the settings-file backstop proven by feeding srt an unscrubbed env directly.
- **Extra coverage:** `ensure_srt` accept/refuse/missing (fake binaries); `run_sandboxed` refuses before spawning when srt is missing (no side effects); missing srt is a failure not a skip (executable statement of the no-skip policy); settings-builder units (allowlist composition incl. URL endpoint, deny-read targets incl. the runs root, credential deny set); clone manager (blob:none config recorded, `origin` URL, named-ref checkout, warm-cache copy, unique run ids, missing-source refusal, idempotent cleanup); `scrub_env` prefix/exact/forbidden semantics.
- **CI round (first push failed on Linux; fixed and green):** srt's Linux network bridges create their unix sockets at `join(tmpdir(), ...)` — with the run-dir TMPDIR in srt's env, the socket path overflowed the kernel's ~108-byte `sun_path` limit and the bridges silently never came up ("Failed to create bridge sockets after 5 attempts" → all four sandbox AC tests red on CI only; macOS passed because its Seatbelt path has no bridges). Fix: `run_sandboxed` runs srt itself with no TMPDIR (node falls back to the short system temp) and gives the child its run-dir TMPDIR as a shell assignment prefix on the `-c` command string. Second push: **both matrix jobs green with the sandbox tests running for real** (run 38922627259 on head `a1c6fb5`).
- **Owner review round (4 findings, all fixed on this PR):**
  - **CI was already green:** the reviewer's bridge-socket guess was exactly the bug found and fixed after the first red push (see the CI-round note above); both matrix jobs pass with the sandbox tests running for real.
  - **Raw-socket test:** `test_direct_socket_to_non_allowlisted_ip_is_blocked` — a child that ignores the proxy env vars and dials `1.1.1.1:443` by raw IP is blocked (EPERM under Seatbelt; no egress at all under bwrap's network namespace). Proxy filtering is no longer the only proven boundary. (Probing also showed `example.com` resolves to 127.0.0.1 on this Mac — a hosts override — hence the fixed IP.)
  - **.git hooks/config escape:** srt already denies writes to `.git/hooks`/`.git/config` of the repo under the child's cwd (found by bisection: the same write succeeds or fails depending only on cwd) — but that protection is cwd-relative, so it's now ours explicitly: `denyWrite` carries the run's `.git/hooks` and `.git/config` as absolute paths, and every orchestrator-side git call (`create_run`'s clone/checkout/rev-parse) runs with `GIT_HARDENING` (`-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false`). Proven three ways: `test_git_hook_and_config_planting_is_denied_in_sandbox` (sandboxed planting denied; normal `.git` writes like COMMIT_EDITMSG still work), `test_planted_hook_never_fires_for_orchestrator_git` (a hook planted directly on disk fires for a plain `git commit` — the control — and never for a hardened one), and a settings unit test.
  - **Env allowlist on every path:** `run_sandboxed(env=...)` no longer merges the caller's dict as a whole env — an explicit env is now an overlay of deliberately chosen names on the same minimal base (`sandbox_env(extra=env)`), so ambient `os.environ` variables cannot cross just because a caller supplied a dict. Proven by `test_provided_env_never_inherits_ambient_variables` (an ambient monkeypatched secret is absent while the chosen extra passes).
  - **CI round 2 (home deny vs srt's own helpers):** the home-wide denyRead broke Linux CI — runners check the repo out under `/home/runner`, so srt's `apply-seccomp` vendor helper (executed from inside the bwrap namespace) became unreadable and every sandboxed command died at startup ("apply-seccomp: No such file or directory"). Fix: `allowRead` now carries the srt package dir (`node_modules/@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`) as well as the run dir — the runtime's own helpers must be visible to the sandbox it creates, while the factory's source and everything else under the repo/home stays denied.
  - **CI round 3 (test-only):** the planted-hook test's control `git commit` failed on the runner — clones carry no git identity and the runner has no global one (masked locally by the developer's `~/.gitconfig`). The test now sets an explicit identity in the run clone, which is exactly what the orchestrator must do per the F-06 note below. Final state: **both matrix jobs green with all 25 F-05 tests running for real on Linux** (run 38928407968, head `6711b4b`).
  - **Home-wide read denial:** `denyRead` now leads with `~` (the run dir is re-allowed via `allowRead`), subsuming `~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh` and `~/factory` in the production layout and closing everything else in the home folder (`~/.aws`, shell rc files, other checkouts); the explicit entries stay for the test layout where the factory root is outside home. Proven by `test_home_reads_are_denied` (a probe file in the real HOME is EPERM inside the sandbox; `/etc/hosts` reads fine). Session-flow consequence recorded for F-06: git identity can't come from `~/.gitconfig` inside the sandbox — pass it via env (`GIT_AUTHOR_*`) or `git -c`, which is the controlled form anyway.
- **Owner review round 2 (10 findings: 4 already fixed by round 1 — the review scored a pre-fix head — and 6 new, all fixed on this PR):**
  - Already-fixed (evidence in round 1 above, re-verified on the current head): `.git/hooks`+`.git/config` denyWrite; home-wide denyRead; `env=` allowlist overlay; raw-socket test.
  - **Timeout orphans (reproduced by construction):** `run_sandboxed` now spawns srt with `start_new_session=True` and, on wall-clock expiry, SIGKILLs the whole process group (`os.killpg`) before raising `TimeoutExpired` — an aborted session can no longer leave its shell/agent processes running unsupervised while the next run starts. Proven by `test_timeout_kills_the_whole_process_group`: a grandchild heartbeat loop stops growing after the timeout.
  - **factory_root guessed from layout:** `build_settings` now takes an explicit `factory_root`; when omitted, the canonical `runs/<project>/<run-id>` nesting is *asserted* — a differently-nested run dir raises instead of silently pointing the holdouts deny at a nonexistent path. Proven by `test_build_settings_rejects_an_unrecognised_run_layout`.
  - **`FACTORY_SRT_BIN` in production:** the override is now honoured **only under pytest** (`PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` set); in production a stale export is inert and the repo-pinned `node_modules` binary is used. A fake that echoes the pin and execs unsandboxed can no longer impersonate srt outside tests. Proven by `test_srt_bin_override_is_inert_outside_tests` (the fake's side effect never fires).
  - **Version check cost:** `ensure_srt` caches the result per binary state (`lru_cache` keyed on path + mtime + size; failures uncached) — one Node boot per process instead of one per sandboxed command. Proven by `test_ensure_srt_caches_the_version_check` (counting fake invoked once across three checks).
  - **`--filter=blob:none` ignored for local clones (reproduced):** plain file:// clones warn "filtering not recognized by server" and materialise fully. `create_run` now passes `--upload-pack "git -c uploadpack.allowFilter=true upload-pack"`, verified empirically: with the override, a two-branch fixture clone contains only the checked-out branch's blobs (1 vs 2). `test_clone_is_actually_partial` asserts the other branch's blob is *absent* via `cat-file --batch-all-objects` — the config assertions alone were never proof. (The first version of this test committed the other-branch file on main, so its blob was legitimately present — fixture bug, not mechanism.)
  - **Warm cache hygiene:** `_relocate_venv` rewrites a copied venv's absolute cache paths (bin shebangs, `activate`, `pyvenv.cfg`) to the run dir, so `.venv/bin/pip` no longer executes the *cache's* interpreter against sandbox-denied paths; `_seed_cache_excludes` appends the cache's top-level entries to `.git/info/exclude` so a session's `git add -A` can't commit `node_modules`/`.venv` even where the target's .gitignore misses them; `.git/info` joins the sandbox's denyWrite so the agent can't undo the excludes. Proven by `test_warm_cache_is_relocated_and_excluded_from_commits`.
  - **"Other projects" read denial, now attempt-proven:** `test_sibling_run_reads_are_denied` reads a real sibling project's run file from inside another run's sandbox → denied (the runs-root deny from round 1, exercised end to end this time).
- **Owner review round 3 (10 findings: the env leak from round 1 correctly re-flagged as unfixed — my round-1 "fix" stopped ambient inheritance but a caller's `env=dict(os.environ)` still crossed — plus 9 new, all fixed on this PR):**
  - **Env allowlist, for real this time (reproduced by the reviewer):** `sandbox_env` now merges ambient + caller env and keeps only `SESSION_ENV_ALLOWLIST` names (PATH/LANG/TZ/TMPDIR, the Anthropic seat vars, git identity, `LC_*`/`GIT_CONFIG_*` prefixes) before the GitHub scrub — so `env={**os.environ, token}` (the natural F-06 call) cannot smuggle AWS/Supabase/OpenAI keys across. Adding a passthrough name is now a deliberate, reviewable edit to one frozenset. Proven by `test_env_dict_of_os_environ_is_allowlisted` (planted ambient secret absent, deliberate session names present). The false "impossible by construction" comment is gone.
  - **Toolchain under $HOME executable (reproduced: rc=126):** the home-wide deny made `~/.local/bin/claude`, pyenv/uv/nvm interpreters — anything F-06 must exec from home — unexecutable. `build_settings(toolchain=...)` (default: this Python + `TOOLCHAIN_BINS` on PATH) resolves each binary and re-allows its directory chain below home (which for a `bin/` dir includes the version prefix where `lib/`/site-packages live). Proven by `test_toolchain_under_home_is_executable` (a real binary under real $HOME runs `--version` inside the sandbox) and `test_toolchain_allowread_covers_version_prefix_and_ancestors`.
  - **Bounded teardown, both directions (both reproduced):** (a) a session leaving `npm run dev &` behind stalled `run_sandboxed` until the background child exited — the group is now SIGKilled after the *normal* leader exit too, so stragglers never outlive the run (`test_background_child_does_not_stall_the_return`); (b) a setsid'd process survives killpg and holds the pipes, and the post-kill drain waited for it (12.4s against a 3s timeout) — the drain is bounded (`_DRAIN_GRACE_SEC`), after which the pipes are force-closed and `TimeoutExpired` propagates (`test_timeout_stays_bounded_against_a_detached_process`). Known residual, documented in the docstring: the escapee process itself may survive (pid-namespace kills are srt/bwrap's side via `--die-with-parent`); the orchestrator never waits for it and its credentials die with the run dir.
  - **HOME is a writable scratch (`<run>/.home`):** the child no longer gets the real, now-unreadable home — pip/npm/Claude Code get a HOME they can read and write inside the run. Git identity can't come from `~/.gitconfig`; it arrives via the allowlisted `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` names. Proven by `test_home_is_a_writable_scratch_inside_the_run`.
  - **`FACTORY_SRT_BIN` gone entirely:** an env var the orchestrator carries (even `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` — itself just an env var) can no longer redirect the sandbox binary. `srt_bin()` is a constant; tests inject fakes via the new `srt_bin_path` parameter on `run_sandboxed` (still pin-checked). Proven by `test_srt_bin_ignores_env_overrides_entirely` and `test_run_sandboxed_takes_an_injected_srt_binary`.
  - **Cache excludes anchored:** entries are now `/name/` — a cache's `lib/` no longer ignores `src/lib/**` everywhere (silent incomplete PRs). Proven by `test_cache_excludes_are_anchored_to_the_run_root` via `git check-ignore` both ways.
  - **Venv relocation handles symlinked cache paths:** both the given and the resolved path are rewritten (`/tmp` vs `/private/tmp`), and `site-packages/*.pth` files join the rewrite set. Proven by `test_venv_relocation_handles_symlinked_cache_paths` (shebang written through a symlinked cache path is relocated). `direct_url.json` and editable-install metadata remain unwritten — recorded as a known limitation for F-06 to revisit if a warm cache ever ships editable installs.
  - **Warm cache refuses tracked collisions:** `git ls-files` top-level components are checked against the cache's top-level entries; a collision (a stray `package-lock.json`) refuses the run instead of silently mutating the checkout. Proven by `test_warm_cache_refuses_tracked_file_collisions`.
- **Owner review round 4 (7 findings — 4 confirmed bugs introduced by round 3, plus scope/docstring issues; all fixed on this PR):**
  - **Pipe deadlock (reproduced: 300KB write ate a 20s timeout):** round 3's `proc.wait()` stopped draining the PIPEs while the leader ran — the classic >64KB pipe-buffer deadlock; real sessions (pytest -v, npm logs, Claude Code streams) would wedge until `timeout_min`. Fixed with a single-threaded select loop: both pipes are non-blocking, every read goes through `select` with a bounded wait, nothing in this process ever blocks on a pipe. Phase 1 polls the leader (≤0.5s latency — a leader that exits while a background child holds the pipes is still noticed and the stragglers killed); phase 2 drains bounded by `_DRAIN_GRACE_SEC`. An earlier reader-thread attempt was abandoned: instrumented, `TextIOWrapper.close()` blocked ~12s on a reader mid-syscall — threads + text wrappers can't be made deterministic here. Proven by `test_large_output_does_not_deadlock` (300KB completes fast, all bytes delivered).
  - **Ambient seat names crossed (reproduced):** round 3's single allowlist let the orchestrator's own `ANTHROPIC_*` and any `GIT_CONFIG_*` ride in by inheritance. Split: `AMBIENT_ENV_ALLOWLIST` (PATH/LANG/TZ + `LC_*` only) vs `SESSION_ENV_ALLOWLIST` (seat + git-identity names, taken **only** from the explicit `env=`). `GIT_CONFIG_*` is on **no** list — env-injected git config is exec-capable (`core.sshCommand`, `credential.helper`); `git -c` on the command line is the controlled route. Proven by `test_ambient_seat_and_git_config_names_do_not_cross`: ambient API key/base URL/GIT_CONFIG_* absent; the explicitly-passed `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` wins over its ambient shadow.
  - **Toolchain reads re-opened ~/.local (confirmed):** the ancestor-chain allowRead re-enabled whole subtrees (uv's credential store, cursor-agent, Copilot shims). Narrowed to **install dir only**, plus the parent when the binary sits in a `bin/` (the pyenv/nvm version prefix where `lib/`+site-packages live). Proven by `test_toolchain_allowread_is_install_dir_only` and — crucially — `test_toolchain_bin_dir_under_home_still_executes`: a real binary under the real `$HOME` still execs, so the round-3 F-06 fix survives its own scope reduction.
  - **`.home`/`.tmp` not git-excluded (reproduced):** scratch (pip/npm caches, Claude Code session transcripts) showed up untracked and would ride into PRs via `git add -A`. `create_run` now seeds `/.home/` and `/.tmp/` into `.git/info/exclude` on **every** run, warm cache or not (`_seed_run_excludes`). Proven by `test_home_and_tmp_scratch_are_git_excluded` (`git status --porcelain` empty after the sandbox writes both).
  - **`_force_close` discarded output:** the select redesign keeps everything read before the grace expiry — `test_timeout_preserves_partial_output` asserts the leader's pre-detach marker survives in `TimeoutExpired.output`.
  - **False "credentials die with the run dir" claim:** the docstring now states the truth — an escaped (setsid) process keeps the model token in its memory and environment for as long as it lives; what it loses is *network* (srt's filtering proxy died), and deleting the run dir revokes nothing. Suspected abuse → rotate the seat token.
  - **fd hygiene (found while fixing, source of the unraisable-exception warnings):** EOF-marked streams leaked their read-end fds, and raw `os.close` raced the buffered file objects at GC. The owning file objects are now closed exactly once each — safe here precisely because the design is single-threaded.
- **Owner review round 5 (3 findings, all fixed on this PR; 1 a regression from round 4's narrowing):**
  - **Claude Code via its `~/.local/bin` symlink was rc=126 again (reproduced on 095ec8b):** narrowing toolchain reads to the resolved install dir dropped the *link* the native installer puts on PATH (`~/.local/bin/claude -> ~/.local/share/claude/versions/X`); exec by that path was denied (rc=126) and by name not found (rc=127) — only the resolved path ran. `_toolchain_allowread` now walks each toolchain path's symlink chain and re-allows every link below home as the literal *file*, never its directory. Proven by `test_toolchain_symlink_under_home_executes` (relative symlink under the real `$HOME`; exec via the link **and** via PATH lookup; asserts the link file is allowed but its dir isn't) — red at rc=126 before the fix. Verified on the owner's Mac against the real CLI: `~/.local/bin/claude --version` and `claude --version` both rc=0 inside the sandbox; default allowRead under home is exactly `~/.local/bin/claude` + `~/.local/share/claude/versions`; `ls ~/.local`, `~/.local/share`, `~/.local/share/uv` all `Operation not permitted`.
  - **`bin/` parent rule could re-open `~/.local`:** a toolchain binary resolving to a real file in `~/.local/bin` re-allowed all of `~/.local`. The rule now adds only the `bin/`'s sibling `lib/` (stdlib + site-packages), never the whole parent. Proven by `test_bin_dir_parent_never_reopens_a_shared_prefix` (`~/.local/bin/x` → exactly `{~/.local/bin, ~/.local/lib}`); `test_toolchain_allowread_is_install_dir_only` updated to the sibling-`lib/` shape. Verified with the real pyenv 3.11.9 interpreter: with only `bin/`, the binary and `lib/` allowed, `import ssl, json, sqlite3, venv` runs inside the sandbox.
  - **Stale docstrings:** `run_sandboxed` still described the abandoned reader-thread teardown; `build_settings(toolchain=)` and `test_toolchain_under_home_is_executable` still described the round-3 ancestor chain. All three now describe the code as it is; `test_run_sandboxed_docstring_matches_the_select_pump` guards the first.
  - Evidence: 50 F-05 tests / 217 total passing locally, ruff clean.
- **Fresh-context review of `d898f10` (follow-up PR; 2 reproduced defects + 1 footgun, all fixed):** an independent session reviewed the merged code against real srt; boundary sound under the production `~/factory` layout, no security hole. Fixed red-first:
  - **F1 — settings credential backstop was exact-name only:** `scrub_env` drops the whole `GH_*`/`GITHUB_*` prefix, but srt's `credentials.envVars` deny takes exact names, so a scrub-bypassing caller leaked e.g. `GH_APP_TOKEN` (reproduced: settings without the name → child sees `prefix-leak`). `build_settings` now denies the well-known names plus every `GH_*`/`GITHUB_*` present in the environment at build time; the module docstring states that limit. `test_settings_backstop_denies_every_github_name_in_the_environment` (red on the missing names; its srt half proven able to fail).
  - **F2 — warm-cache copy followed symlinks:** a dangling or circular (pnpm-style `self -> ..`) link aborted the run, and a venv's `bin/python` was copied as a 6 MB file. `copytree(symlinks=True)`; absolute links into the cache are retargeted into the run; `_relocate_venv` never rewrites through a symlink. `test_warm_cache_symlinks_are_preserved_not_followed`; checked with a real `python -m venv` cache (link kept, `sys.prefix` is the run's copy).
  - **P3 — `python` missing from the default toolchain:** `which python3` returns a pyenv *shim script*, so adding the name alone would allow the shim, not the interpreter. `_python_toolchain()` asks each `python3`/`python` on PATH for its `sys.executable`. `test_python_toolchain_follows_shims_to_the_real_interpreter`.
  - Not fixed, recorded: P1 (`sandbox_env` used with srt directly on Linux reintroduces the deep-TMPDIR bridge-socket failure; `run_sandboxed` is the supported path), P2 (`.git/info` denyWrite blocks in-sandbox git maintenance like `update-server-info`; commits unaffected).
  - Evidence: 53 F-05 tests / 220 total passing locally, ruff clean; real `claude --version` by name still rc=0 in the sandbox.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13, with the sandbox tests running for real on Linux) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-06 — Session runner (Claude Agent SDK on Z.ai)

- **Commits:** `feat/f06-session-runner` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite failed on the missing `factory.session` module before implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-16. Interactive session; real-session evidence gathered the same day (smoke + blocked-write runs below).
- **Design notes:** new `factory/session.py`; small extensions to `factory/sandbox.py` and `factory/runs.py`.
  - **SDK pinned exactly:** `claude-agent-sdk==0.2.168` in `pyproject.toml`. The runner is written against the 0.2.x `ResultMessage` — `model_usage`, `terminal_reason`, `api_error_status` — which 0.1.x (previously installed globally) drops entirely. CI installs the pin via `pip install -e ".[dev]"`, so a drift is a red run.
  - **Seats:** `implementer` runs `permission_mode="bypassPermissions"` — the srt sandbox *is* the permission system (DESIGN D3), prompts are meaningless unattended, and PreToolUse deny hooks still block under bypass (verified live in AC-3). `reviewer` disallows `Write`/`Edit`/`NotebookEdit` outright (WO: "the reviewer gets no Write/Edit"; NotebookEdit is the same class). Seat system prompts are a `SeatConfig` field left empty until F-09.
  - **Env:** `session_env()` returns *literally* the three seat names (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `API_TIMEOUT_MS`) — it reads nothing ambient, so no GitHub token, keychain secret or inherited `ANTHROPIC_*` can appear in options by construction. `setting_sources=[]` on every seat.
  - **Hooks (in-process, via `HookMatcher`):** `Write|Edit|NotebookEdit` denied when `file_path` matches a `paths.protected` glob — matched in three forms (as-given, cwd-joined absolute, cwd-relative), fnmatch semantics (``*`` spans `/`, erring protective). The callback also re-checks `tool_name` (defence in depth for direct use). `Bash` denied on `\bgit\s+push\b`, the standalone word `gh` as a command (`(?:^|[\s;&|/])gh(?:\s|$)` — plain `\bgh\b` false-positives on "gh-gh-gh", verified), and the `.config/gh` substring.
  - **Clock:** each message receive runs under `asyncio.wait_for(…, timeout=remaining)` with a single deadline — a stream that keeps yielding is still cut off at the limit. Option building and record extraction sit outside the clock (the WO's "paused outside the session").
  - **Record:** `SessionRecord` from the `ResultMessage`: `num_turns`, `subtype`, `is_error`, `terminal_reason`, `usage` tokens, `total_cost_usd`, `result`; model name from `model_usage` (the entry with the most input+output tokens when several ran; all names in `models`). `to_dict`/`from_dict` for the JSON boundary.
  - **Sandboxed execution (`run_session_sandboxed`):** execs the entry under `run_sandboxed` with the profile-built settings; outer wall clock = `timeout_min` + teardown grace so the in-session clock fires first. Two F-05 extensions: `build_settings(allow_read=…)` for deliberate read carve-outs, and `default_toolchain(extra=…)` extracted from the old inline default (behaviour unchanged; F-05 suite untouched-green).
  - **The staged runner copy (found by a real failure):** the first probe ran the entry as `python -m factory.session` from the repo — and imported the *clone's* `factory/sandbox.py`. Root cause, isolated by instrumenting a real sandbox: python's import must **list** the directory that holds a package, the sandbox denies listing the repo, so `import factory` silently fell through sys.path to the `factory/` package in the target clone (untrusted shadowing; a self-hosting clone of the factory repo hits the same way). Fix: the orchestrator stages a copy of the factory package at `<settings>-runner/` — outside the run dir (the agent can write nothing there), re-allowed for reading via `allow_read`, importable because its *parent* is exactly what's allowed — and the entry is a `-c` bootstrap inserting that dir ahead of cwd. The repo itself stays list-denied, so prompts/schemas/validator docs remain unreadable to sessions. `cleanup_run` removes the `-runner` sibling with the settings file. `PYTHONPATH` is on no allowlist (the bootstrap needs none); a test pins that it never crosses, either ambient or explicit.
  - **Token:** `seat_token()` = keychain item `factory-zai` (production path per DESIGN D2) → ambient `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (the owner's current bootstrap env). No token → `SessionConfigError` with the one-liner fix, before anything spawns.
  - **Token-gated real tests (recorded for review):** AC-3's real half and AC-4 need a credential that by design exists only on the orchestrator's Mac (Rule of Two; CI is Linux). Where `seat_token()` is None — the CI runner — those tests assert the runner's *refusal* path instead: a deliberate distinction from the F-05 no-skip rule (a credential that must never leave the Mac is not a broken stack), and the refusal itself is real, executed behaviour — the entry-refusal test runs the actual `python -c <bootstrap>` process, SDK import and all. Both shapes run the full suite green locally (below).
  - **`permission_denials` is empty for hook denials** (verified live): the CLI populates that field for `can_use_tool` denials, not PreToolUse hook denies. AC-3's real half therefore evidences the attempt by turn count (a denied Write costs a turn) plus the absent file — and uses an ordinary new file (`protected.md`) as the target rather than a sandbox-also-denied path like `.git/config`, where the srt backstop would mask a hook failure (a positive control: hook broken ⇒ file appears ⇒ test fails).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_1` — an injected query that never finishes (it keeps yielding, so the clock must bound the *stream*, not just silence) returns `aborted`/`wall_clock` in 2–15 s against a 3 s budget.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — `error_max_turns`, `error_during_execution`, `is_error` + `api_error_status` 429, and a mid-stream `CLINotFoundError` are each returned as a failed record with the reason in it; the runner never raises.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — unit half: deny matrix across Write/Edit/NotebookEdit × relative/absolute/`.git` forms, allow cases (`src/app.py`, a Read on a protected path), bash-guard deny/allow matrix (incl. `/usr/bin/gh`, `git  push` double-space, the `gh-gh-gh` false-positive guard); real half (owner's Mac): a real sandboxed session ordered to Write `protected.md` completes with the file absent and ≥2 turns — the hook denied a genuinely attempted write.
- **AC-4** proven (owner's Mac, 2026-10-16): `test_ac_4` — a real session inside the real srt sandbox against `https://api.z.ai/api/anthropic` completed "Reply with exactly the word ok" in 1 turn, `result == "ok"`, and the recorded model — `claude-opus-5-5`, from `model_usage` — is the Z.ai Coding Plan's Opus→GLM mapping showing up exactly as DESIGN D2 predicted ("don't hard-code a mapping… record the actual model"). CI shape: the refusal path, per the token-gating note above. Owner step ("F-00 step 1 answered before AC-4 is run repeatedly or on a schedule"): F-00 step 1 was owner-confirmed 2026-10-16, before this one-off run — compliant; the step stays for reconcile to tick, and repeated/scheduled runs remain gated on it.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `setting_sources == []` on every seat; `options.env` is exactly the three seat names while the ambient environment carries `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `JFO_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` and a foreign `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`; the reviewer seat disallows the edit tools, the implementer seat doesn't.
- **Extra coverage:** record distillation (multi-model `model_usage` → primary by token weight), a stream that ends with no `ResultMessage` → `failed`/`no_result_message`, unknown seat → `SessionConfigError`, the entry's no-token refusal (real subprocess, exit 2, fix message, no traceback), the bootstrap's clone-shadowing immunity (poison `factory/` in cwd loses to the staged copy), `_stage_runner` (no `__pycache__`/pyc, restages clean, idempotent), `cleanup_run` removes the `-runner` sibling, `SessionRecord` JSON round-trip, PYTHONPATH never crosses into the sandbox, hook output shape pinned to the SDK's `SyncHookJSONOutput` contract.
- **Evidence:** 16 F-06 tests / 236 total passing locally in **both** shapes — without a token (the CI shape: refusal halves) and with the owner's Z.ai token (real sandboxed sessions against Z.ai, ~$0.24 of API across the runs) — ruff clean.
- **Owner review round on PR #18 (9 findings — 7 owner-reproduced, 2 code-reading; all fixed on this PR, each with a red-first test in `tests/test_f06.py` under `test_review*`):**
  - **(1) Direct `run_session` calls leaked the whole orchestrator environment** (owner-reproduced; verified in the SDK transport: it merges ALL of `os.environ` into the Claude Code subprocess — `options.env` is an overlay, never a replacement). Fix: `run_session` **windows the process env** for the stream's life — `_window_environ` keeps only process mechanics (PATH/LANG/TZ/HOME/TMPDIR, `LC_*`) plus the proxy names (inside the sandbox those carry srt's authenticated proxy, the session's only network route) and the seat names, and the finally restores the orchestrator's env exactly. Proven by `test_review1_direct_call_does_not_leak_ambient_environment`: GH_TOKEN, an AWS key, a foreign `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and a planted secret are absent for the whole stream (checked again after the first message), PATH survives, and everything is restored afterwards.
  - **(2) Default settings path one level too shallow** (owner-reproduced): `run_dir.parent.parent` is the *runs root*, so settings landed at `<factory>/runs/sandbox/…`, which `cleanup_run` never looks at — every session leaked a settings file plus a staged factory copy. Fix: derive `runs/<project>/<run-id>` → `<factory>/sandbox/<project>-<run-id>.json` with the layout **asserted** (same refusal style as `build_settings`), so the derived path always equals `RunDir.settings_path`. Proven by `test_review2_default_settings_path_uses_the_factory_root` (no `runs/sandbox` dir is ever created) and `test_review2_settings_and_runner_are_cleaned_up` (real `create_run`/`cleanup_run`: settings, `-runner` copy and spec all gone).
  - **(3) Protected-path hook bypasses** (owner-reproduced): `tests/../schema/x`, `./schema/x`, `Schema/x` and `/private/var` symlink-prefix spellings all passed, and Bash writes weren't checked at all. Fix: paths are matched as a cross-product of textual forms — as-given, `normpath` (collapses `./` and `a/../`) and fully `resolve`d (symlink prefixes) — crossed with the given and resolved cwd, **case-insensitively on both sides** (macOS filesystems are case-insensitive; lowercasing also over-denies `Schema/x` on Linux, which errs safe). Bash write targets are now checked too: redirections (`>`, `>>`, `2>`), the common write utilities (tee/mv/cp/rm/install/truncate/shred/unlink/sed -i) and output flags (`curl -o`, `wget -O`, `--output=…`) — best-effort, explicitly documented as a deny-layer rather than a shell parser, with L0's diff policy as the authoritative protected-path gate. Proven by `test_review3_protected_path_traversal_and_case_are_denied` (4 traversal/case forms + the /var→/private/var pair) and `test_review3_bash_writes_to_protected_paths_are_denied` (7 denied writes; ordinary commands and writes elsewhere allowed, including `mv schema/x src/x` — a move *out of*, not into, the protected tree).
  - **(4) Bash guard evasion** (owner-reproduced): `git -C . push` (the old regex required git and push adjacent), `"gh"` (quoted), `` `gh` `` and `$(gh …)` all passed. Fix: git-push is denied when `git` and `push` appear as separate words anywhere in the command (covers `-C`/`--git-dir`/`-c` options between them; over-denies e.g. `echo git push` — the safe direction); the gh check runs on a **de-quoted** command (quotes and backticks stripped) with `(`/`)` added to the word boundaries so command substitutions match. Recorded with the reviewer's own framing: the structural barriers are that the sandbox holds no GitHub token and has no network route to github.com — this hook is the in-session policy layer, not the boundary. Proven by `test_review4_bash_guard_covers_optioned_and_quoted_forms` (7 forms denied; the earlier allow matrix — `git status`, `git commit`, `echo gh-gh-gh`, `ls .config` — still passes).
  - **(5) Sessions ran the auto-updating `~/.local/bin/claude`** instead of the pinned CLI. The SDK's own `_find_cli` prefers its bundle; my `_resolve_cli` override defeated it. Fix: `_bundled_cli()` returns the native binary inside the pinned `claude_agent_sdk` package (verified shipped in the macOS **and** manylinux wheels, so CI has it too); `run_session_sandboxed` runs it and **refuses** (`SessionConfigError` naming the pin to reinstall) rather than silently falling back to an unpinned CLI — the auto-discovering `_resolve_cli` is gone. Explicit `cli_path` still wins for tests only. Proven by `test_review5_sandboxed_sessions_use_the_sdk_bundled_cli` (spec names the bundle; the bundle is in the toolchain; no `~` anywhere in the path) and re-proven live: AC-4's real session now runs the bundled CLI inside the sandbox.
  - **(6) `.session-spec.json` landed in the clone as an untracked file** (owner-reproduced: a `git add -A` would commit the prompt and home paths). Fix: the spec lives inside the staged runner dir — outside the run dir entirely, next to the factory-package copy, allow-read with it and removed by the same `cleanup_run` step. Proven by `test_review6_spec_never_lands_in_the_clone`: after a prepared session, `git status --porcelain` in the clone is empty and the spec exists outside it.
  - **(7) Ambient `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` sent to Z.ai without checking its base URL** (code-reading). Fix: `seat_token()` uses the ambient token only when the ambient `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` names the Z.ai endpoint (trailing-slash tolerant); a token exported for another provider — or with no base URL at all, i.e. api.anthropic.com — is not ours to send. Proven by `test_review7_ambient_token_requires_the_zai_base_url` (three cases).
  - **(8) Wall-clock abort never closed the SDK stream** (code-reading). Fix: `_close_stream` awaits `aclose()` (bounded, exceptions suppressed — closing is cleanup, never the failure being reported) on the abort path, the SDK-error path, and normal completion. Proven by `test_review8_abort_closes_the_stream`, whose fakes are plain iterators, not async generators — loop shutdown cannot auto-close them, so the closed flag proves the runner itself closed the stream on both paths.
  - **(9) `_primary_model` crashed on null token counts** (code-reading). Fix: null counts weigh zero (`int(x or 0)`), empty usage → `None`. Proven by `test_review9_primary_model_tolerates_null_counts`.
  - Commit discipline: no AI-attribution trailers, matching the round's commit.
- **Evidence (after the review round):** 27 F-06 tests / 247 total passing locally in **both** shapes — without a token (CI shape) and with the owner's Z.ai token (real sandboxed sessions against Z.ai running the **bundled pinned CLI**, ~$0.20 additional API spend) — ruff clean.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. The owner step above is answered in substance (F-00 ledger, 2026-10-16); ticking it in `tasks.json` happens at reconcile.
- **Owner review round 2 on PR #18 (4 findings — 1 new bug, 2 hardening, 1 convention; all fixed on this PR, red-first under `test_review10/11/12`):**
  - **New bug — concurrent sessions stripped the orchestrator's env** (owner-reproduced): `_window_environ` swaps the whole process env, so a second overlapping session saved the first's *stripped* copy and restored it last — `GH_TOKEN` gone for good; a future F-10 loop that ever overlapped sessions would silently lose its GitHub token. Fix (the reviewer's "refuse a nested window" option): a module-level `_WINDOW_OPEN` flag, checked and set at non-await points (single-threaded asyncio — no interleaving can race it), refusing a second window with a `SessionConfigError` naming the serial rule. Sessions in one process are serial; the F-10 loop is. Proven by `test_review10_overlapping_sessions_refuse_and_keep_the_env`, which reproduces the exact strip ordering (second window opened inside the first, first restores, second restores *last*) and asserts the refusal plus `GH_TOKEN` intact — red on the old code ('ran' instead of 'refused').
  - **Non-SDK errors skipped the stream close** (owner code-reading): the close sat on three exit paths; a non-SDK raise (which propagates by design — a bug must stay loud) leaked the CLI subprocess. Fix: the close moved into the existing `finally`, so every exit path — completion, abort, SDK error, unexpected raise — closes the stream (while the env window is still up) and restores the env and flag. Proven by `test_review11_non_sdk_errors_still_close_the_stream`: a plain iterator raising `RuntimeError` propagates *and* its `closed` flag is set.
  - **Bash guard residue** (owner-reproduced, low impact by their own framing — no token, no github.com route): `sh -c 'git pu''sh'` and `g\it push` passed. Fix: the de-quote table now strips backslashes as well as quotes/backticks, and the git/push word check runs on the de-quoted command (previously only gh did) — both forms deny. The claim is stated precisely now: this hook is a best-effort deny-layer on top of the structural barriers, not an evasion-proof boundary. Proven by `test_review12_bash_guard_backslash_and_sh_c_quoting` (3 forms incl. `g\h pr create`).
  - **Commit subject missing `(F-06)`** (CLAUDE.md convention): the round-1 commit was amended to `fix: env window, pinned bundled CLI, hardened hooks, cleanup paths (F-06)`; round 2's commit carries the tag from the start.
- **Evidence (after review round 2):** 30 F-06 tests / 250 total passing locally (no-token shape — nothing model-facing changed this round, so no API spend), ruff clean.
- **Owner review round 3 on PR #18 (1 finding — the round-2 git/push word-anywhere check was too broad; fixed on this PR, red-first under `test_review13`):** the owner's cases (`git commit -m 'fix push notification retry'`, `git log --grep=push`, `grep -rn push src/`, `echo gh is the cli`) were all denied — a session working on push-notification code couldn't commit truthfully or search its own code. Fix, per the owner's direction ("split the command into its separate parts and deny only when one part actually runs git push or gh"): the de-quoted command is split into **invocations** (`_bash_invocations`: on `;` `|` `&` newlines `()` `<>`), and `_invocation_violation` denies only when an invocation's program is git whose **subcommand** is `push` (global options `-C .`/`-c k=v`/`--git-dir=…` skipped first, so `git commit`/`git log`/`git help push` pass) or `gh` itself; shells' `-c` payloads (`sh -c 'git pu''sh'`) and prefix wrappers (env/command/exec/xargs/nice/nohup) recurse. Proven by `test_review13_push_or_gh_mentions_in_ordinary_commands_pass` (7 allow cases — red before the fix) and `test_review13_real_invocations_still_deny` (16 deny cases: every round-1/2 evasion form plus `/usr/bin/git push`, `env git push`, `command gh`, `bash -c 'gh auth login'`). **Residual, stated in the docstring and PR:** pipes feeding a shell's stdin (`echo git push | sh`), git aliases and unenumerated wrappers still pass — the hook is a best-effort deny-layer over the structural barriers (no GitHub token, no network route to github.com), not an evasion-proof boundary.
- **Evidence (after review round 3):** 32 F-06 tests / 252 total passing locally (no-token shape; guard-only change, no API spend), ruff clean.
