### F-42 owner review (PR #89): pattern precision and the push ordering

Four findings, all fixed on the same branch (`fix: attribution patterns and pre-push PR check (F-42)`), red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review4`.

1. **`generated with` matched the phrase anywhere.** The probed ordinary lines ("docs: client SDK generated with openapi-generator", "Fixture data generated with faker", "report generated with ruff") failed L0 on legitimate text. The footer pattern now requires the phrase followed — within two connecting tokens, so "Generated with GitHub Copilot" reaches its tool — by an AI tool (bracketed or bare: `[Claude Code]`, Claude, Copilot, Cursor, Codex, Gemini, ChatGPT, GPT, OpenAI, Anthropic) or an AI vendor link, anywhere in a line (a title carries it mid-line too). The ordinary-prose lines name no AI tool and pass; the real footer lines still fail.
2. **The PR-text refusal fired after the push.** The check lived inside `gh_pr_create`, downstream of `push_branch`, so a refusal stranded a remote branch under `pr_create_failed` — and the body's fenced sections (implementer notes, the validator JSON) legitimately QUOTE trailers when reviewers discuss attribution. New `factory.publish.check_pr_text(title, body)`: the title scans unanchored (a title is one line, so a trailer anywhere in it is deliberate — `T-1: add Co-Authored-By: Claude support` refuses), the body scans free text only (fences stripped, an unclosed fence stays scanned — fail closed); the orchestrator calls it BEFORE `push_branch` (a refusal is the deterministic `publish_refused` class, no orphan branch), and `gh_pr_create` keeps the same rule as its backstop. Squash inheritance stays closed: the squash message comes from the title (checked) and the branch commits (L0-checked).
3. **Bare ambiguous names flagged humans.** `Co-authored-by: Kimi Larsson <kimi@acme.se>` failed — a real colleague credited illegitimately. The name list is split: unambiguous product names (Claude, Anthropic, Copilot, ChatGPT, GPT-n, Codex, OpenAI, the factory's own seat names) match alone; names that are also human names (Kimi, Gemini, Mistral, Llama, Cursor, Qwen, GLM, Moonshot) flag only with an AI shape beside them — a bot/noreply address, an AI vendor address (moonshot.ai, deepseek.com, mistral.ai, claude.com/ai joined the vendor domains), or the product's compound name ("Cursor Agent", "Gemini Code Assist"). A human Kimi at their company address passes; `Kimi <bot@moonshot.ai>` fails.
4. **Other trailer styles slipped through.** `Assisted-by:`, `Generated-by:` and `AI-assisted:` now follow the same rules as `Co-Authored-By` (one shared trailer-prefix alternation, so a rule change applies to all four), and the bare `AI-assisted: yes` declaration fails on its own — that trailer exists only to declare AI assistance.

Patterns were restructured once, not patched four times: one core list (`_PATTERN_CORES`) compiled anchored (line-initial trailers/declarations for commit messages and bodies, where prose may discuss a trailer) and unanchored (titles) — never two hand-maintained lists. Two implementation defects the red tests caught mid-round: the anchored prefix class originally absorbed the newline itself (empty matched lines; now `[^\w\n]{0,8}`, which also correctly absorbs the footer's emoji), and the title needed the unanchored form rather than a widened prefix (a widened prefix would have unanchored commit messages too, tripping prose that discusses trailers). `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` L0 states the widened rule. Red on the pre-fix tree (`bb87c84`): all four review tests failed on the behaviour their finding names — the probe lines hit (or passed) the wrong way through `attribution_match`, and review2 failed on the missing `check_pr_text` surface with the branch already pushed. Green: 13 f42 tests; full gate **843 passed**, ruff clean.

Second review round (PR #89): `tests/test_f42.py::test_review5_mid_line_footer_and_suffixed_model_names` red on `dd4ef9d` — a mid-line "generated with Claude Code" in a commit body passed L0, `aider (gpt-4o)` and `cursoragent@cursor.com` co-authors passed. Fix: the footer core matches unanchored in every surface (its AI-tool requirement keeps prose out), `gpt-?\d\w*` covers suffixed model names, `cursor.com` joins the vendor domains. Green: 14 f42 tests; full gate **844 passed**, ruff clean.

## F-43 — Egress redaction seam

- **Commits:** `feat/f43-egress-redaction` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f43.py` landed first and failed on the gaps each AC names — AC-1 on the literal seat token reaching the rendered PR body through the real `run_once` publish path (the C-1 hole itself), AC-2/AC-3 on `TypeError` (`check_pr_text`/`flush_vents` took no `secrets`), AC-4 on `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'factory.egress'`, AC-5 on the JWT shape passing `redact_credentials` unredacted, AC-6 on "orchestrator.py:2772: implementer notes bypass the seam".
- **Built:** 2026-11-05. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/prbody.py`'s input path, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/session.py`, `factory/trace.py` are protected; `factory/publish.py`, `factory/vent.py`, `factory/status.py` rode the same owner direction — the seam touches them all) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-43, CODE-REVIEW-2026-11-05 C-1/M-8/m-16/I-10, docs/remediation-plan-2026-11-05 §F-43, `factory/trace.py`, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/prbody.py`, `factory/publish.py`, `factory/vent.py`, `factory/status.py`, the orchestrator's PR-body path and trace setup, `factory/session.py`'s stdout-dump path, `tests/test_f33.py`, the test_f42 `_Env`/`_Seats` harness, `.factory/project.yaml`).
- **Design notes:** ONE seam, `factory/egress.py::scrub_session_text(text, secrets=())` — literal scrub (`scrub_literal`, one per caller-known secret), then `redact_credentials`, then `strip_control_sequences`, in exactly that order (literals first so no later rewrite can leave a fragment behind; the strip last so redaction judged the text as written). The signature takes a SEQUENCE of secrets, never "the seat token": F-59's per-provider credentials thread through the same parameter. `scrub_literal`/`MIN_SECRET_LEN`/`REDACTED` moved from `factory/trace.py` to `factory/sentinels.py` (beside `redact_credentials`, so the seam composes one home); `factory.trace` re-exports them unchanged — one implementation, and `factory/session.py`'s existing import keeps working. `scrub_session_value` is the recursive form for report dicts (keys included — a hostile finding dict can carry a secret in a key the body renders); it scrubs a projection and never mutates the stored reports. Scrub-then-cap everywhere the PR body cuts (F-27's invariant): the implementer notes are seam-scrubbed whole, THEN capped at `_PR_NOTES_CAP`; the reports are seam-scrubbed, then `_compact` cuts the tails. `check_pr_text` is deliberately NOT the seam: a second, independent fence (AC-2) that scans title AND body — fences included — for every caller secret and refuses, naming the refusal without echoing the token; it assumes the seam may have failed, so it never depends on it having run. Vents are seam-scrubbed TWICE (the F-27 invariant): in-sandbox at `VentCollector.record` (the default collector now carries the session's token) and again at `flush_vents` (the orchestrator hands `egress_secrets`); the unparsable-record dump (`_save_session_stdout`) takes the session token and writes seam-scrubbed text. Stripping is at PRINT, not at write (AC-4's contract line): the stored trace/progress log keep their content — the seam's control-strip applies to what a terminal executes: `read_trace` (also the dashboard's SSE/run page follower) and `render_status` (CLI print and dashboard page) strip at their one reading/render seam; vents are the deliberate exception — the contract says vent text passes the full seam, so stored vents are stripped at the flush (the collector already stripped in-sandbox; the flush is idempotent). `egress_secrets = (seat_token() or "",)` is computed once per run beside the `TraceWriter` construction and feeds both, so no channel scrubs a different secret set.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f43.py::test_ac_1` — the real `run_once` pipeline (clone manager, queue, L0/L1 through the pinned srt sandbox, real `build_pr_body`, real `check_pr_text`, real `push_branch` against a bare remote; only the seats scripted, `orchestrator.seat_token` planted) publishes with the literal token AND a bare `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<jwt>` echo planted in the implementer's result and in a passing review's one minor finding: neither reaches the rendered body, the finding still renders with `[redacted]` (the path was exercised, not skipped), and the echo's value is pattern-redacted (`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=[redacted]`).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — `check_pr_text` with the RAW (never seam-scrubbed) body refuses `PushRefused` naming "literal session credential" without echoing the token or its value; the title refuses too; clean text passes; a literal below `MIN_SECRET_LEN` is noise, not a refusal.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — `flush_vents` with `secrets` writes the task's progress log with neither the token, nor the echoed JWT, nor any `\x1b` byte on disk; the in-sandbox `VentCollector(secrets=...)` records the same scrub before anything crosses the boundary; `_save_session_stdout(..., secrets=...)` writes the dump without token/echo/control bytes while keeping the ordinary text ("rewritten" survives — the seam shreds commands, not content).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — `strip_control_sequences` removes the three m-16 shapes (screen clear `\x1b[2J\x1b[H`, OSC-8 hyperlink with ST terminator, cursor move `\x1b[5A\x1b[?25l`) keeping surrounding text and `\t`/`\n`; `read_trace` over a trace file carrying an OSC-8 link prints no escape byte; `render_status` over a vent logged through the seam renders the link's text with no `\x1b` anywhere on the page.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — for each new shape (`JSON web token`, `bearer token`, `unquoted YAML password`): `redact_credentials` removes the value, the `credential_added` sentinel fires on an added line naming the shape without echoing the value, and a fresh F-33 attack diff (`corpus/attack/credential_added-{7,8,9}.patch`) flags through the real replay under this repo's real risk globs. The benign corpus false-positive count: **0 before, 0 after** (measured 2026-11-05 by replaying all 41 benign patches through `patch_findings` against `.factory/project.yaml`'s globs; `tests/test_f33.py` 25/25 green both sides).
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — the channel list derived by grepping the writers, pinned structurally: (1) every `build_pr_body` call scrubs BOTH session-text inputs (`implementer_notes` via `scrub_session_text`, `reports` via `scrub_session_value`); (2) a `check_pr_text` call passes `secrets`; (3) every `flush_vents` call passes `secrets`; (4) `TraceWriter` takes `extra_secrets`; (5) `session.py`'s `_save_session_stdout` call passes the token. The tripwire: any call in `factory/orchestrator.py` receiving session-derived text (`.result`/`.reason`/`.vents`/`.findings`) whose callee is not the seam, a guarded writer, a local-disk sink (attempt record/queue/progress-log findings/stored reports/trace writer), a prompt builder (ingress into the next session — the review's own function), or a pure expression op, fails naming file:line — a NEW channel cannot silently skip the seam. Detection verified by mutation: planted `print(impl.result)`, `gh_pr_comment(union.findings)` and `print(record.vents)` each trip it (checked against the AST logic, 2026-11-05). The terminal/off-machine reading seams (`read_trace`, `render_status`) are pinned by AC-4's behaviour tests plus the dashboard's own contract that both go through them.
- **Hostile pass (pre-commit):** planted-token probes against every channel. **One real leak found and fixed red-first**: a token SPLIT BY A CONTROL SEQUENCE (`<half>\x1b[0m<half>`, and the same inside the env echo's JWT) survived the literal scrub — not the literal while split — and the strip then MERGED the halves into the whole token after every scrub had run. Fix: the strip is itself a text-rewriting boundary, so when it changes the text the scrubbers re-judge the rewritten form (no-op cost when nothing was stripped); the contract's composition order stands, `test_hostile_control_split_secret_does_not_reassemble` pins it. Blocked as designed: the raw literal in notes/finding/vent/dump, the bare `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<jwt>` echo, quoted, `Bearer <token>`, unquoted YAML `password:`, a control-split inside an L2 finding, a token in a finding KEY / parse_error / dropped / verifier-dropped text (the recursive scrub covers keys), and a token inside a fenced PR-body block (the seam scrubs the input BEFORE `prbody` builds the fence, and `check_pr_text` scans fences for literals — probed live). Known limits, unchanged from pre-F-43 and out of this contract: a token split across newlines (the seam keeps line structure; the trace writer has had the same limit since F-30) and an encoded token (base64 — no shape list decodes; L0's diff scan and F-59's provider work own that class).
- **Also changed (owner-directed in this interactive build):** protected judge/gate modules `factory/sentinels.py` (three credential shapes; `scrub_literal`/`MIN_SECRET_LEN`/`REDACTED` moved in, re-exported from `factory/trace.py`), `factory/trace.py` (the re-export; `read_trace` strips at print), `factory/orchestrator.py` (`egress_secrets`; seam at the PR-body build; `secrets` to the fence and every vent flush), `factory/session.py` (collector carries the token; the dump takes `secrets`), `factory/publish.py` (`check_pr_text` literal fence), `factory/prbody.py` UNTOUCHED (the seam wraps its inputs at the orchestrator call site — the builder stays pure). Not protected, same direction: `factory/vent.py` (seam at record and flush), `factory/status.py` (strip at render). New: `factory/egress.py`, `tests/test_f43.py`, `corpus/attack/credential_added-{7,8,9}.patch`. `.factory/project.yaml` gains `tests/test_f43.py` under `paths.protected` (the PR #93/test_f16 class of gap, closed in the same PR). `.factory/tasks.json` untouched — no approve, no reconcile (owner's, after merge). Open recommendation for the owner: `factory/egress.py` itself is not yet a `paths.protected` entry — behaviorally guarded by the protected `tests/test_f43.py`, but the F-42 `factory/attribution.py` precedent suggests listing it; left out here because the owner's instruction named only the test file.
- **Evidence:** 7 F-43 tests (6 ACs + the hostile-pass finding test) / F-33 corpus gate 25/25 (3 new attack diffs covered by its glob) / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **977 passed** (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; scripted seats — no model spend, no real sessions this WO).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` via `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` once this PR's gate is green and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. Next Tier 1 WO: F-44.

### F-43 owner review (PR #98): ten findings — all fixed on the same branch, red-first

`fix: egress seam review round 1 (F-43)`; each finding has `tests/test_f43.py::test_review1..test_review10`, landed red on the round-1 head (`277746e`) before its fix (red evidence per test in the PR conversation; review9's red counts were `{'value': 9, 'text': 3}` — 3 report keys × 3 shrink iterations). **Scope extension (owner-blessed, recorded as F-27 AC-4 recorded its re-scope): F-43's contract said "every orchestrator path", but C-1's intent is every path session text leaves by — the importer is in scope from this round.**

1. **Importer wrote session text to `tasks.json` unscrubbed (critical: the owner commits and pushes it; JobFinderOS is public).** `run_import` now computes `egress_secrets = (seat_token() or "")` and passes the parsed proposal through `scrub_session_value` BEFORE `plan_import`/validation — every session-derived string (titles, sources, AC texts, owner steps, keys included) loses the token, the shapes and the control bytes first, so a redaction can never produce a schema-valid-looking but different task without the plan diff showing it. `record.reason` in `sdk_import_session`'s abort refusal is seam-scrubbed too (the message prints to the terminal).
2. **`_save_refused_output` was pattern-only — the exact M-8 gap.** It takes `secrets` and writes `scrub_session_text`; `keep_refused_import_output` and `_abort_refusal` thread them through (the run_import except path, the sdk abort sites with the token, and the CLI's own apply-refusal keep with `seat_token()`).
3. **The AC-6 tripwire parsed only orchestrator.py.** Now `_EGRESS_MODULES`: orchestrator, importer, session, cli, eval, status, vent — derived by `grep -lE '\.result\b|\.reason\b|\.vents\b|\.findings\b|\.raw_output\b|proc\.stderr|proc\.stdout' factory/*.py`, keeping every module where the hits are model-session values (dropped as plumbing over its own results: gates/sandbox/mergeorder/toolkit/publish/rehearsal — CommandResult and exception plumbing; l2 — parse/merge whose outputs reach egress only through the guarded reports channel). Taint attributes and names are checked separately (a variable *called* `result` is not session text until one of its session fields is touched). Mutation-verified again: a planted `print(proposal['title'])` in importer.py and `print(result.raw_output)` in cli.py both trip (`test_review3`).
4. **`factory/egress.py` and `factory/vent.py` were unprotected.** Both added to `paths.protected` citing the F-42 `factory/attribution.py` precedent: a factory-built PR cannot weaken the seam (scrub returning its input, clean_text dropping its secrets) without the protected-path check forcing a human review.
5. **`SessionConfigError` embedded 2000 chars of raw runner stderr.** The no-record message now seam-scrubs with the session's token first, then caps (F-56 AC-2 will generalize this to the abort paths — noted there).
6. **8-bit C1 controls (U+0080–U+009F) passed the strip.** The C0/DEL class gains `[\x80-\x9f]`; `\u009b2J`, `\u009d8;;…\u009c` links and `\u0090` DCS go, while å/ä/ö/emoji survive (the strip removes control bytes, not characters).
7. **An unterminated DCS/SOS/PM/APC introducer swallowed everything after it** — a hostile seat could hide its own findings from the rendered body while the seam reported success. The ST terminator is now REQUIRED: without one, only the two-byte introducer falls through to the catch-all and the text after it stays.
8. **No shape for unquoted env echoes.** `environment variable echo` joins `_CREDENTIAL_SHAPES` (UPPERCASE name ending `_TOKEN`/`_KEY`/`_SECRET`/`_PASSWORD`, `=`, 16+ unquoted value — the Z.ai key is `<id>.<secret>`, not a JWT, so no shape caught it and only the literal scrub did). Both `redact_credentials` and the sentinel get it (one tuple); `corpus/attack/credential_added-10.patch` is its attack diff; the AC-1/AC-3 planted echoes now carry BOTH a JWT-shaped and a Z.ai-shaped value. **Benign corpus: 0 false positives before and after** (all 41 patches, real replay, real risk globs).
9. **The reports were re-scrubbed on every tail-shrink retry.** The scrub is hoisted above the `for tail in (...)` loop (scrub once, compact the scrubbed copy — compaction only shortens already-scrubbed text); counts pinned at one notes call and one call per report key, the publishable body unchanged.
10. **`check_pr_text` re-implemented `scrub_literal`'s guard.** `factory.sentinels.is_scrubbable_secret` is the one rule; both `scrub_literal` and the fence call it, so F-59 cannot change one without the other.

**Hostile pass (this round):** the importer probed end-to-end — title, AC text, `source` path, `owner_steps` text, `risk` (rejected by the schema enum before anything else), refused save, printed plan — with the literal token, a Z.ai-shaped `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<id>.<secret>` echo and control bytes: clean everywhere when the importer holds the echoed key (the production shape). C1 and DCS edges probed through the full seam: C1 CSI/OSC/DCS, a C1-split token (re-judge catches it — `[redacted]`), unterminated `ESC P`/`ESC _`/OSC with a finding and a token after them (text survives, token redacted), a terminated DCS wrapping the token (removed whole). Known limits, unchanged in class: a bare unrecognized secret value the caller does NOT hold (my first probe leaked exactly this by planting two different keys — in production `egress_secrets` is the session's own key, and the named echo forms are shaped); a lone partial token fragment with its other half absent (inert); newline-split and encoded secrets (F-30 class). Full detail in the PR.

**Evidence (this round):** 10 review tests (17 total in `tests/test_f43.py`) / importer + corpus + vent + profile suites green / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **987 passed** (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; scripted sessions — no model spend, no real sessions).

**Review round 2 (PR #98, head `e9d3078`): one defect, introduced by round 1 — fixed both levels, red-first (`tests/test_f43.py::test_review11_cli_keep_scrubs_literal`, `test_review12_str_secrets_refused`, and `test_review3` tightened).**

- **The bug:** `factory/cli.py`'s apply-guard keep path passed `secrets=(seat_token() or "")` — a parenthesised STRING, not a tuple. The seam iterated it character by character; every character is shorter than `MIN_SECRET_LEN`, so the literal scrub never ran and the refused output saved on that path kept the seat token in clear. `test_review3`'s round-1 rule only asserted the keyword was PRESENT, so the suite stayed green. Fixed to `secrets=(seat_token() or "",)` — and a package-wide grep for `secrets=(`/`extra_secrets=(` without a tuple comma found exactly this one site (`factory/importer.py`, `factory/session.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/trace.py` and every test site are tuples or pre-built names).
- **The class:** a bare `str` is now refused, loudly, at EVERY entry that takes `secrets`/`extra_secrets` — `factory.egress.check_secrets` is the one guard (`TypeError: secrets must be a sequence of secrets, not a str — … pass a tuple/list, e.g. secrets=(token,)`), called by `scrub_session_text`, `scrub_session_value`, `check_pr_text`, `clean_text`, `flush_vents`, `VentCollector.__init__`, `_save_session_stdout`, `_save_refused_output`, `keep_refused_import_output`, `_abort_refusal` and `TraceWriter.__init__` (the F-30 `extra_secrets` has the same hazard — covered). Tuples and lists both pass.
- **The rule:** `test_review3`'s CLI/importer secrets assertions now require the keyword's VALUE to be a sequence shape (tuple/list literal, a name, or an expression containing one) — a bare parenthesised expression fails it. Mutation-verified: reverting the fix to the string shape trips the rule at `cli.py:490`.
- **Evidence:** red on `e9d3078` — review11 "the literal seat token survived the CLI keep path", review12 "DID NOT RAISE TypeError", review3 "secrets is missing or not a sequence"; green after the fix (19 tests in `tests/test_f43.py`). Full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **989 passed** (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; scripted sessions — no model spend).

**Review round 3 (PR #98, head `6b7dea8`): six findings — fixed red-first (`tests/test_f43.py::test_review13`, `test_review14` (parametrized ×3), `test_review15`, and `test_review3` rewritten).**

1. **scrub_session_value discarded the guard's tuple** (the important one): `check_secrets(secrets)` ran, its normalised tuple was thrown away, and the recursion carried the RAW argument — a one-shot iterable (a generator, F-59's lazily-built credential view) was consumed by the guard and every nested scrub, and the top-level string case, then saw nothing: silent no-scrub, the exact class round 2 set out to remove. Fixed: `secrets = check_secrets(secrets)` once, recursion through a private `_scrub_value(value, secrets)` that takes the checked tuple (also stops re-tupling per report-tree node). Proven: `test_review13` — a generator through `scrub_session_value({"k": …, "l": […]})`, and through `scrub_session_text`, `check_pr_text`, `flush_vents`, `VentCollector`, `TraceWriter`, `_save_refused_output` and `_save_session_stdout`, scrubs or refuses everywhere (red on `6b7dea8`: "the generator was consumed by the guard").
2. **check_secrets did not validate elements**: bytes (what F-59 keychain reads will produce) passed `is_scrubbable_secret` and died inside `str.replace` with an error that never named the cause; a nested tuple the same. The guard now refuses any element that is not `str` or `None`, naming the element's TYPE only — never its value, which may be the secret. `None` passes (the `(token,) if token else ()` shapes). Proven: `test_review14` — `(b"x"*16,)`, `((token,),)` and `(16,)` each refused naming bytes/tuple/int with no value in the message; `("", None, token)` accepted.
3. **The never-mask contract**: a guard `TypeError` replaced the diagnostic the three save helpers exist to preserve. Now: `keep_refused_import_output` chains explicitly (`raise guard_error from exc` — it HOLDS the original as a parameter, so `__cause__` is exact); `_save_session_stdout` and `_abort_refusal` are raised while the caller handles the original, so it stays reachable as `__context__` (documented — the guard's `TypeError` is a programming error that deliberately wins, never silently). All three docstrings say exactly that. Proven: `test_review15` — each helper called while handling a planted `ValueError` raises `TypeError` with the original reachable (`__cause__` for keep — red on `6b7dea8`, `__context__` for the save helpers).
4. **Guard inventory** (simplification): DIRECT — `scrub_session_text`, `scrub_session_value` (the seam), `check_pr_text` (no seam), `VentCollector.__init__`, `TraceWriter.__init__` (they store secrets), `_save_session_stdout`, `_save_refused_output` (writers), `keep_refused_import_output` (the finding-3 exception: its guard is the point where `from exc` chains), `flush_vents` and `_abort_refusal` (each can COMPLETE without ever forwarding — zero vents, no scratch proposal — so the refusal must be direct; `test_review12`'s empty-vents and no-scratch cases pin exactly this). INHERITED — `clean_text` (its guard removed; it always forwards to `scrub_session_text`). `test_review12` passes unchanged: every listed entry still refuses a bare str.
5. **The static secrets-shape rule was too weak**: any subtree containing a tuple passed, and any bare Name passed — `(seat_token() or "") if real else ()` and `secrets=<str-bound name>` both survived. `_is_secrets_expr_ok` now accepts only a tuple/list literal, a `check_secrets(...)` call (the guard's own normalisation — a tuple by construction), an IfExp whose BOTH arms are acceptable, or a Name the same module assigns only from acceptable shapes (parameter pass-through only for the names `secrets`/`extra_secrets`). Mutation-verified: the round-2 string shape, the str-armed IfExp with a tuple else-arm, and `secrets=project` (a str-bound name) each trip, named file:line.
6. **The rule now covers the whole package**: every `secrets=`/`extra_secrets=` keyword and every `egress_secrets =` assignment in every `factory/*.py` — the rule's first run caught two real pass-throughs in importer.py that only resolved once `check_secrets(...)` assignments were recognised as proven sequences. Mutation-verified: dropping the comma in orchestrator.py's `egress_secrets = (seat_token() or "",)` trips, naming `factory/orchestrator.py:<line>`.

**Evidence (this round):** 24 tests in `tests/test_f43.py` (red on `6b7dea8`: review13 "the generator was consumed by the guard", review14 ×3 DID NOT RAISE, review15 `__cause__ is None`, review3's package-wide rule on the two importer pass-throughs) / adjacent suites 124 passed / full gate below.

## F-44 — Verdict integrity

- **Commits:** `feat/f44-verdict-integrity` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f44.py` landed first and failed on the four defects the work order names — AC-1 on the raw `"  Blocking "` being stored (`assert '  Blocking ' == 'blocking'`), AC-2 on the conflicting verdict parsing as clean (`assert None is not None` on `parse_error`), AC-3 on `parse_test_count` reading the forgery (`assert 999999 is None`), AC-4 on the removed `with pytest.raises` block flagging nothing (`assert 'assertion_removed' in []`). All five hostile probes red the same way (below).
- **Built:** 2026-11-05. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/l0.py`, `factory/l2.py`, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/eval.py` are protected — this WO's four changes sit exactly on the judge/gate paths, the F-42/F-43 precedent) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-44, CODE-REVIEW-2026-11-05 M-1/m-10/m-8/I-11, docs/remediation-plan-2026-11-05 §F-44, `factory/l2.py` whole — the parse/validation, `_extract_json`, `parse_reviews_with_retry`, `merge_reviews`, every `.strip().lower()` site — `factory/eval.py::_count_blocking`, `factory/l0.py::parse_test_count`/`_TEST_COUNT`/the min_tests floor, `factory/sentinels.py::assertion_removed` and its patterns, `tests/test_f33.py` corpus, `tests/test_f38.py` and `tests/test_f23.py` whole — the merge and retry contracts this WO must not break).
- **Design notes:** AC-1 canonicalises severity ONCE at parse, `severity = severity.strip().lower()` beside the axis canonicalisation in `_validated_report`; the stored `L2Finding` (and everything rendered from it — PR body, attempt record, drops) carries the canonical value. The module docstring already claimed "the canonical value stored"; the code now does it. Seven redundant defensive copies removed from `factory/l2.py` — `_fails_round` (was l2.py:138), the in-loop blocking check (:443), `merge_reviews`' blocking check (:864), `_default_tag` (:975), `_unanimous` (:984), `apply_vote`'s blocking-tag check (:1068) and nit-budget check (:1084) — every `L2Finding` flows from `_validated_report` (the only production constructor), so all seven compare canonical spellings directly now. `eval._count_blocking` KEEPS its case-insensitive read (made case-insensitive — it was exact `== "blocking"`, M-1's second half): it reads STORED report dicts, and records written before F-44 may hold `"Blocking"`. AC-2 refuses output carrying more than one valid verdict object with differing verdicts as `_InvalidVerdict` — the existing retry path (one re-run per seat, `parse_reviews_with_retry`) is the only mechanism, no second one added; repeated objects with the same verdict stay accepted (the last-positioned candidate is returned, the F-09 selection rule for agreeing candidates); only dicts whose top-level `verdict` is pass/fail count as verdict objects, so a verifier vote object never conflicts. AC-3 reads the count from the runner's own summary LINES — pytest's bordered final line, pytest `-q`'s bare tail, jest's `Tests:` line — collecting every `N passed` claim on them: no claims → None; disagreeing claims → None (the runner prints exactly one count, so differing claims are a forgery mid-run or from atexit/a session-finish hook after the real summary — "which line is last" is unknowable, both are untrusted); agreeing claims → the count. The floor treats None as NOT cleared (verified through `run_l0`, not assumed — `test_count_unknown`). AC-4 adds `\bpytest\.raises\b|\braises\s*\(` to `ASSERTION_PATTERNS` (both the `pytest.raises` attribute form and the `from pytest import raises` bare form; a full symbol alias `raises as expect_exc` evades any line scan — the same limit a renamed `assertEqual` always had, and why L2 reviews the diff) and the same pattern to `_ASSERTION_STATEMENT`, so a `pytest.raises` block MOVED within a file removes one with-line and adds one — moves stay quiet exactly as moved asserts do, keeping the F-25/F-33 net-loss arithmetic intact. `self.assertRaises`/`assertRaisesRegex` were ALREADY covered: the broad `\bassert` matches the "assert" inside them (the `.` before it is a word boundary) — pinned by test so the decision is explicit.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f44.py::test_ac_1` — `"  Blocking "` parses clean and is STORED as `blocking` (axis precedent unchanged); the M-1 scenario both merge orders (reviewer 1 `"Blocking"` + reviewer 2 `"minor"`, both declared pass) merges to ONE entry, severity `blocking`, `reported_by=[1,2]`, union verdict FAIL (pre-F-44: rank-99 "Blocking" lost to "minor" and the union PASSED — the red run shows it); `_count_blocking` counts `"Blocking"` and `"blocking"` and not `"minor"`/`"note"`/None.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — FAIL-stated-then-example-PASS and the reverse both refuse (`parse_error` names the conflict, verdict fail, never a pass); repeated same-verdict objects accepted; the retry harness gives the conflicting seat exactly ONE re-run (`retries == 1`, the reason names the conflict) and a still-conflicting retry keeps the seat invalid; fail-closed traced through the pipeline — `merge_reviews` yields the invalid FAIL with `parse_error` set, `apply_convergence` keeps fail (the `vote_rejected_fail` pass-flip requires `parse_error is None`), `apply_vote` keeps fail with a well-formed vote.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — the three summary shapes (`==== 46 passed ====`, `-q`'s `46 passed in 0.05s`, jest's `Tests: 2 failed, 5 passed, 8 total`); no summary → None; a printed `999999 passed` mid-output → None (m-8); two claims inside ONE summary line → None; agreeing repeats → the count; the floor verified through the real `run_l0`: no readable count with a `min_tests` baseline → `test_count_unknown`, verdict fail, and the m-8 defeat at floor level (fake 999999 before the real `3 passed`, floor 100) FAILS where pre-F-44 it PASSED (red evidence: `assert 999999 is None`).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — the with-form block (`with pytest.raises(ValueError):` + non-assert body) removed → `assertion_removed`; the call form `pytest.raises(fn, E)` removed → flagged; `assertRaises`/`assertRaisesRegex` removals flagged (already covered, now pinned); a same-file move stays quiet; the fresh attack diff `corpus/attack/assertion_removed-5.patch` flags through the real replay under this repo's real risk globs. **Benign corpus: 0 false positives before, 0 after** (all 41 patches replayed through `patch_findings` against `.factory/project.yaml`'s globs with the old pattern set and the new one, 2026-11-05; the four pre-existing `assertion_removed` attacks still flag).
- **Hostile pass (pre-commit):** severities — mixed case and whitespace canonicalise; unknown scales (`critical`, `blocker`, `high`, `urgent`, `info`) stay INVALID verdicts, never silently minor (`test_hostile_severity_shapes`). Verdict JSON — a fenced example that DISAGREES with the stated verdict is refused in either object order (`test_hostile_verdict_json_inside_a_fenced_example`, `test_ac_2`); one that AGREES is an echo; an example quoted inside a finding's TEXT string never becomes a candidate (JSON escaping keeps the fence off the candidate list — probed, parse clean, verdict stands). Fake counts — before the summary (mid-run print, real `pytest -q -s` subprocess): the fake is a summary-shaped claim, disagrees, count → None; AFTER the summary (real `atexit` probe): the atexit print is provably the LAST line (`output.strip().endswith(...)` asserted) yet never wins — disagree → None, so the atexit attack is DEFEATED, not a known limit; a session-finish hook print lands BEFORE pytest's own summary and glues onto the `-q` progress line (`[100%]999999 passed...`) — not a summary-shaped line, ignored, the REAL count (2) stands: either hook outcome fails closed for the forger, and the fake count can never become the floor's count (`test_hostile_fake_count_printed_mid_run`, `test_hostile_fake_count_printed_after_the_summary`); two claims inside one summary line → None (`test_ac_3`). pytest.raises evasions — indentation and helper moves are line-text-unchanged, the substring scan holds; `from pytest import raises` (bare form) and `pytest as pt` aliases (`pt.raises(`) are caught (`\braises\s*\(` matches after the dot); **known limit:** a full symbol rename (`from pytest import raises as expect_exc`) evades any line scan — same class as a renamed `assertEqual`, L2's diff review owns it (`test_hostile_raises_removal_rewritten`). Known limit, unchanged from pre-F-44: a verdict object broken by an unescaped in-string fence example can still parse as the example alone (the outer object was never a candidate — identical pre-F-44 behaviour, out of this contract).
- **Also changed (owner-directed in this interactive build):** protected judge/gate modules `factory/l2.py` (severity canonicalisation + the seven redundant lowercasing removals; `_extract_json` conflict rule; docstrings), `factory/l0.py` (`_TEST_SUMMARY_LINE` + `parse_test_count` final-summary read), `factory/sentinels.py` (`ASSERTION_PATTERNS` + `_ASSERTION_STATEMENT` gain the pytest.raises forms), `factory/eval.py` (`_count_blocking` case-insensitive). `tests/test_f09.py::test_review6_the_real_verdict_wins_over_an_earlier_example` RENAMED and rewritten to `test_review6_conflicting_verdict_objects_are_invalid`: it pinned exactly the last-wins rule m-10 convicted ("the inverse of the example-then-verdict case the docstring optimizes for"), and F-44's remediation plan names both orders refused — the F-42 precedent of re-pinning a test_f09 expectation test when its contract changes, cited in the test; the queue's F-09 acceptance refs are `test_ac_N` only, nothing references the old name. New: `tests/test_f44.py` (protected from the day it exists — `.factory/project.yaml`, the test_f28/F-43 line), `corpus/attack/assertion_removed-5.patch` (+ one line in `corpus/README.md`). `.factory/tasks.json` untouched — no approve, no reconcile (owner's, after merge).
- **Evidence:** 9 F-44 tests (4 ACs + 5 hostile probes) / F-33 corpus gate green incl. the new attack / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1003 passed** (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; scripted seats — no model spend, no real sessions this WO).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` via `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` once this PR's gate is green and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. Next Tier 1 WO: F-45.

### F-44 owner review (PR #100): seven findings — all fixed on the same branch, red-first

`fix: verdict integrity review round 1 (F-44)`; findings 1/2/3/5 red-first under `tests/test_f44.py::test_review1..test_review5`, findings 6/7 pinned by `test_review6`/`test_review7` (behavior already correct — the pins exist so it cannot silently regress).

1. **All-skipped suite + atexit forgery cleared the floor (confirmed bypass).** The round-0 rule collected only lines containing "passed", so when the real result line had no passed count (`2 skipped in 0.00s`), a forged `999999 passed in 0.01s` from atexit was the ONLY claim and became the count. Reproduced against real pytest (exit 0, zero tests run, `parse_test_count` → 999999 — red output in the PR). Fixed by the final-result-line rule (finding 3): the honest result line reads **0** (no passed count reported → 0, never None and never the forgery), and the after-it forgery is a SECOND result line → ambiguous → None. `test_review1` runs the real repro.
2. **Anchored shapes missed honest output (confirmed regression).** Colour (`PY_COLORS=1`, also FORCE_COLOR/CI/addopts `--color=yes`) wraps the tail in ANSI — `^\s*\d+ passed` never matched, every floored task read `test_count_unknown`; and `-q` with a failure leads with `1 failed, 1 passed in …`, which does not start with "N passed". Both reproduced against real pytest 9.2 (`test_review2`). Fixed: control sequences are stripped first — `factory.egress.strip_control_sequences`, the one stripper, imported at l0's top (egress imports only sentinels — no cycle) — and the bare shape accepts any leading parts (`passed|failed|skipped|deselected|xfailed|xpassed|warnings?|errors?`, or `no tests ran`), plus xdist's parenthesised human duration (`… in 374.65s (0:06:14)`), which would otherwise have broken this repo's own gate floor.
3. **Re-implemented at the root: the FINAL RESULT LINE rule.** Exactly one result-shaped line → its passed count (0 when it reports none); zero result lines → None; MORE than one → None (a forgery after the real one or a second suite — "which is last" is unknowable, so neither is trusted); two passed claims on the one line → None (not a readable result); a printed bare `N passed` that is not result-shaped is not the runner's line — the honest result stands. `test_review3` pins every leg; the round-0 mid-run and in-line-double-claim probes stay green under it (mid-run full-shape fake → two result lines → None; bare count print → ignored, honest count; in-line double claim → unreadable result line → None).
4. **Multi-suite commands: option (a) — one runner per test command.** Chosen over summing: reading first/last/max invents a count no single runner stated, and summing would let a suite that dropped to zero hide behind its sibling; ambiguity fails closed to `test_count_unknown`, which a human reads. Documented in `parse_test_count`'s docstring and as the `commands.test` description in `schema/project.schema.json` (protected, owner-directed). `test_review4` refuses two real-content-separated result lines as ambiguous and pins the schema note.
5. **The replacement-side raises pattern was unanchored.** `result = parse(x)  # was pytest.raises` (trailing comment) and `check = pytest.raises` (alias assignment, no call) counted as replacement assertions — removing the real block and adding one such line levelled the count and defeated AC-4 (both now flag; red before). The entry is anchored `^(?:with\s+)?(?:pytest\.)?raises\(` and matched on the string-blanked line with any trailing `#` comment cut — blanking runs FIRST, so a `#` inside a quoted string never truncates code. The REMOVED side stays broad by design. Real statement forms (with/call/from-import) still count as replacements — moves stay quiet. **Benign corpus: 0 false positives with the round-0 pattern and 0 with the anchored one** (41 patches, real replay, real globs; all five assertion_removed attacks still flag).
6. **Conflict set vs nested objects — probed, cannot happen, pinned.** `_balanced_objects` yields only OUTERMOST spans (a span is appended when depth returns to 0), so a finding's structured evidence carrying its own `"verdict"` key is never a candidate: probed with a self-consistent FAIL review carrying `"evidence": {"verdict": "pass"}` — one span, `parse_error` None, verdict fail. `test_review6` pins exactly that, so a future scanner change that starts yielding nested spans fails the pin instead of silently voiding honest reviews.
7. **The re-pin's original concern stays covered.** `test_f09.py::test_review6_conflicting_verdict_objects_are_invalid` gains the FAIL+FAIL agreeing leg (PASS+PASS was already there; `test_ac_2`'s AGREEING fixture is fail+fail through the retry harness): an agreeing example quoted before the real verdict parses AS THAT VERDICT, `parse_error` None, and `test_review7` proves no rerun is spent on the clean parse.

**Evidence (this round):** 7 review tests in `tests/test_f44.py` (16 total) + the FAIL+FAIL leg in `tests/test_f09.py` / adjacent suites `test_f07`+`test_f33`+`test_f41`+`test_l0_review` green (168 passed together), `test_f25`+`test_l2_blank_missed_earlier`+`test_l2_review_fanout` green (80 passed) / benign corpus 0→0 / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1010 passed**.

### F-44 owner review, round 2 (PR #100): the in-process forgery and the AC-2 fail-open

Two findings on `9c094bf`, both reproduced against real pytest before any fix — the round-1 seven verified. Fixed red-first on the same branch (`test_review8..test_review14`), one commit.

1. **The in-process runner forgery defeats AC-3's parser (sentinel fix).** Test code runs inside the runner's process: it prints a result-shaped line and hard-exits (`os._exit(0)`) before pytest prints the real one — the forgery is then the ONLY result line and the exit code is the test's. Reproduced (real pytest, exit 0, failing suite): the test-file probe via `capsys.disabled()` and the conftest probe via `pytest_sessionfinish` + `os._exit(0)`; `parse_test_count` returns **999999** on both. No stdout parser can close it — the fix is a sentinel. **`runner_exit_forgery`** (factory/sentinels.py, the fifth built-in): ADDED lines in test files (conftest included — TEST_GLOBS lists it) matching one pattern tuple, each entry commented with why — hard exit/kill (`os._exit(`, `os.abort(`, `sys.exit(`, `raise SystemExit`), capture escape (`capsys.disabled(`, `capfd.disabled(`, `sys.__stdout__/__stderr__`, `os.write(1|2,`, `getplugin("capturemanager")`), and runner session hooks defined in test code (`def pytest_sessionfinish/terminal_summary/unconfigure`). It escalates exactly as `assertion_removed` does: an L0 finding and a failed sentinel row — a human decides. **Second honest-diff narrowing, found by the gate itself**: the full suite's `tests/test_f10.py::test_ac_11` seat-diff adds an honest `pytest_terminal_summary` to a `tests/conftest.py` (F-05's sandbox probe) and failed the new sentinel — hooks now flag in TEST files only, where pytest never registers them (dead code no legitimate test writes); a conftest is the registered plugin surface, and there the exit/capture patterns carry the forgery load (the round-2 conftest probe flags on its `os._exit` line, and a bare conftest hook print before the summary is already handled by the parser's result-line rule). Pinned in `test_review10`; `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` states the carve-out. **Kill narrowed per the corpus rule**: `os.kill(` flags only non-self kills (`(?!os\.getpid\()`) — self-kill is the honest crash-simulation shape benign PR #22 carries (a scripted seat killed mid-session, inside a heredoc string the line blanker cannot see); killing another pid (the runner — `getppid`) is the attack shape and flags. A `pytest_sessionfinish` in a repo's EXISTING conftest is not an ADDED line and never flags (`test_review10` proves the removed-only case). `tests/test_f25.py`'s `_SENTINEL_CASES` gains the flagged/clean pair (the set-equality contract demands it — `os._exit(0)` flagged, `with pytest.raises(SystemExit):` clean); `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` L0 §4 lists the fifth built-in; three fresh attack diffs (`corpus/attack/runner_exit_forgery-{1,2,3}.patch`, one per family) — **benign corpus 0 false positives** (41 patches, real replay, real globs, five-sentinel set). `test_review9` runs BOTH real-pytest probes, pins the parser limit honestly (still 999999 — that is why the sentinel owns it), and asserts the diff introducing each probe flags through the new scan.
2. **AC-2 still failed OPEN when the reviewer's real verdict object did not parse (owner addendum).** All four shapes returned `pass`/`parse_error=None`: a dup-key real FAIL beside a parseable pass example, a truncated one, a trailing comma, and a FAIL stated only in prose after the example — the example was the only parseable candidate, so it won. Closed fail-closed in `_extract_json`, same single retry path, three rules: (a) a verdict-shaped candidate (fence or balanced span mentioning "verdict") that fails to parse beside a parseable one → invalid, the reason naming the candidate's LINE, never its content; (b) a TRUNCATED verdict object never closes — so it is neither fence nor span — a verdict-bearing line with an unclosed `{` after the chosen object that does not parse alone → invalid; (c) the chosen object must be the LAST verdict-bearing content: prose AFTER it that states a verdict (a line naming "verdict" and exactly one of pass/fail, or a standalone PASS/FAIL line) → invalid; the protocol echo (`"pass" or "fail"` names both, states neither) never fires. **Goal-attack beyond the four** ("can a FAIL the reviewer meant ever publish as PASS?"): the case-4 MIRROR — prose FAIL BEFORE the only parseable object — still published as PASS, so before-text now invalidates on CONTRADICTION only (agreeing prose before the object is an echo; `test_review14`); `Overall: FAIL` after a well-formed fail object and a `## Verdict: PASS` heading before one both held; a fenced standalone FAIL after the object held; a "verdict … must fail" prose line held. **Known limits, recorded plainly**: (i) a homoglyph keyword (`VЕRDICT` with Cyrillic Е) evades the prose rule and publishes the example's PASS — a text scan without a confusables table cannot close it; L2's diff review owns that class, and the object-level protocol is unaffected (JSON verdict values are exact). (ii) Prose after the object that mentions "verdict" and one keyword on a line invalidates even when it narrates rather than states ("the verdict section notes a build that did fail once") — fail-closed by design, one retry. (iii) The line scan itself is evadable (`getattr(os, "_e" + "xit")`, importlib, exec) — L2 owns it; **the real fix is an out-of-band result file the factory reads instead of stdout** (pytest `--junitxml` / jest `--json` to a factory-owned path, counts cross-checked against the result line, a missing file failing closed) — being drafted as a new F-46 acceptance criterion, deliberately NOT built here.

**Evidence (round 2):** `test_review8..test_review14` red on `9c094bf` for every behavior they name (review11/12: the four fail-open shapes returned pass; review8/9/10: no sentinel existed), then green — 24 tests in `tests/test_f44.py` / `tests/test_f33.py` 25/25 with the fifth sentinel and the three new attacks / `tests/test_f25.py` green with the new `_SENTINEL_CASES` pair / `tests/test_f10.py` green under the conftest hook carve-out / benign corpus **0** false positives (41 patches, five-sentinel replay) / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1017 passed**.

### F-44 owner review, round 3 (PR #100): goal closure — seven findings, all reproduced at `1f59ad6`, fixed red-first (`test_review15..test_review21`)

1. **AC-2 still failed open (blocking).** The round-2 prose rule keyed on the word "verdict", so the commonest LLM phrasings — `**FAIL** — …`, `Overall: FAIL`, `Result: FAIL`, `## FAIL`, before or after the example — all published the example's PASS. Re-decided at depth (`_stated_verdicts` v2): every line's word-bounded pass/fail keyword tokens, markdown emphasis/heading/quote/bullet marks stripped first (`**FAIL**`, `` `FAIL` ``, `__FAIL__`, `## FAIL`, `- FAIL`, `> FAIL`, `1. FAIL` all state their verdict), a line naming both keywords stays the neutral protocol echo, and CONTRADICTION-only applies on BOTH sides of the chosen object — an agreeing token anywhere is an echo (which also fixed finding 5: an honest "Verdict: PASS — clean diff." after a pass object stays clean). Adversarial pass over the rule: 14 after-shapes + the before-mirror + both contradiction directions all invalidate; agreeing restatements, protocol echoes, password/failover/passing/fails prose all stay fine. **Known limits** (ledger + L2-owned): negation ("does not pass"/"not a pass" beside a fail object reads as a contradicting pass token → one retry — a false FIRE, fail-closed, never a wrong publish); gerund/participle statements ("I'm failing this", "The migration failed") carry no keyword token and evade; homoglyph (`FАIL`), zero-width and leetspeak (`F41L`) evasions remain (no confusables table in a line scan).
2. **Conftest outcome flip forged a real pass (blocking).** A conftest `pytest_runtest_makereport` hookwrapper assigning `rep.outcome = "passed"` turned `assert 1 == 2` into "1 passed", exit 0, with **no finding** — pinned against real pytest in `test_review16` before the fix. Rework: OUTCOME-REWRITING hooks (`runtest_makereport`, `report_teststatus`, `runtest_logreport`, `runtest_protocol`) and outcome assignments (`.outcome =`, `.exitstatus =`, `.testsfailed =`, `==` comparisons excluded) flag ANYWHERE in test files or conftest — the conftest is the live surface. Session/reporting hooks (`sessionfinish`, `terminal_summary`, `unconfigure`, `collection_modifyitems`) stay test-files-only: they cannot flip an outcome, the conftest is their honest registered surface (test_f10's sandbox probe), and the parser's result-line rule plus the exit/capture patterns carry the load there. Corpus `runner_exit_forgery-3.patch` replaced — the inert test-module hook fixture is now the WORKING conftest makereport forge. Benign replay: **0/41** with the round-3 patterns.
3. **Dead pattern.** `getplugin("capturemanager")` matched a quoted string against the string-blanked line — could never fire (probed: `[]`). Replaced by `\bsuspend_global_capture\b` (the dangerous call, quoted nowhere); `test_review17` is the demanded per-pattern parametrisation — EVERY entry in all three tuples fires on its own example line, so a dead pattern cannot return unnoticed.
4. **False-fail: quoted code voided honest PASS.** `_FENCE` captured ```python blocks with the tag inside the candidate, and the malformed rule fired on any unparsable fence mentioning "verdict" — every dogfood review of this repo's own l2.py tripped it. Fences tagged with a language other than json are no longer candidates (`(?![\w-])` after the optional tag), and the malformed rule requires an OBJECT shape (`{`-leading + "verdict"). `test_review18` proves the honest code quote parses clean while round 2's three malformed cases still invalidate.
5. **False-fail: agreeing restatement voided.** After-text rejected any stated verdict; now contradiction-only everywhere (see 1). `test_review19`.
6. **Self-kill carve-out's dependency pinned.** `os.kill(os.getpid(), …)` exempts killing the runner's own process — safe only because the gate fails on the signal exit. Pinned at both levels in `test_review20`: a real pytest killed by its own test exits **-9** (returncode < 0, real subprocess), and a signal-exit test command FAILs `run_l0` with `command_failed` naming -9. The same reasoning now covers the JS mirror (`process.kill(` non-self only).
7. **JS shapes.** `process.exit(`, `process.kill(` (non-self), `process.stdout/stderr.write(` joined the tuple — a jest target's sentinel row is no longer a claim of coverage that cannot fire. No JS benign evidence exists yet (this repo is Python-only; JobFinderOS onboards the first, per corpus/README).

**Out of scope, restated:** the out-of-band result file (pytest `--junitxml` / jest `--json` at a factory-owned path, counts cross-checked against the result line, missing file failing closed) is the real fix for findings 2 and 6 and stays drafted as a new F-46 acceptance criterion. **Dogfood note:** a real factory run over this repo's own F-44 diff will show `runner_exit_forgery` findings on `tests/test_f44.py`'s own added fixture lines (`rep.outcome = 'passed'` etc.) — by design: the finding escalates to a human, and this PR is that review.

**Hostile re-read of my own rule (pre-commit):** the first cut of `_stated_verdicts` tokenized raw lines, so a FAIL object beside CITED CODE containing `"pass"` (`if value.get("verdict") == "pass":` — the finding-4 dogfood shape, mirrored) voided the honest review. Fixed by blanking quoted spans per line first (the sentinels module's one blanker, imported): a quoted keyword states nothing, an unquoted one (`**FAIL**`, `` `FAIL` `` after markdown strip) still does. Known limit this buys: a verdict stated only inside quotes in prose (`Verdict: "FAIL"`) evades — contrived beside the four named shapes, L2-owned.

**Evidence (round 3):** `test_review15..test_review21` red on `1f59ad6` (every goal shape published/evaded as the review describes), green after — 31 tests in `tests/test_f44.py` / `tests/test_f33.py` 25/25 incl. the replaced attack / `tests/test_f25.py` + `tests/test_f09.py` + the l2 suites green / benign corpus **0** (41 patches, round-3 patterns) / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1024 passed**.

### F-44 owner review, round 4 (PR #100): a regression fixed, the goals honestly restated

The owner re-ran every round-2/3 repro at `fb66730` — all seven round-3 findings held — then showed both GOALS still fail against ordinary inputs, and ruled the scope: fix the regression this PR caused, correct the ledger, and route the rest to their owners (the nonce redesign and the conftest-hook policy are F-46+ work, not F-44's contract).

1. **The apostrophe regression (fixed, `test_review22`, red on `fb66730`).** Round 3's hostile-re-read fix blanked quoted spans with the CODE blanker, whose pattern treats `'` as a quote mark — so "It's a FAIL — the reviewer's call stands." (and "can't approve this: FAIL", "doesn't meet the spec. Verdict: FAIL") blanked the FAIL and published the example's PASS; the last shape was INVALID at `1f59ad6`, i.e. round 3 reopened a closed one. Fix at the root of the regression: prose blanking covers **double-quoted spans only** (`_DQ_SPAN`) — a cited `"pass"` states nothing, an apostrophe is an apostrophe. Adversarial pass on the fix (12 shapes not written for): six apostrophe-verdict phrasings (`reviewer's FAIL`, `don't ship: fail`, `Couldn't merge: FAIL`, `users' tests fail`, `I'd give it a fail`, `It's a **FAIL**`) all invalidate; cited double-quoted code beside a FAIL object, agreeing restatement and the protocol echo stay clean. New known limits this buys: a verdict stated only inside DOUBLE quotes in prose evades; single-quoted cited code (`== 'pass':`) false-fires (one retry, fail-closed). L2 owns both until the nonce work order.
2. **Ruff warning fixed** — the comment above `LINT_SUPPRESSION_PATTERNS` spelled out a noqa directive verbatim; reworded. `ruff check .` fully clean, no warnings.
3. **Ledger corrections (this entry is the record).**
   - **Goal (a) is NOT met.** Closed: every round-2/3/4 probed FAIL-statement shape except the both-keyword contrast lines added below (objects, malformed objects, truncation, dup keys, prose before/after, markdown emphasis, headings, list items, apostrophe prose). Still open, all against a quoted in-band example, all owned by a future **nonce work order** (proposed: the factory issues a per-run nonce in the reviewer prompt; only a nonce-carrying object is a verdict — the prompt's example uses a placeholder and no nonce, so it can never count; multiple/malformed nonce-bearing objects fail closed): `FAILED —`, `Rejected:`, homoglyph (`FАIL`)/zero-width/leetspeak keywords, gerund/participle statements ("I'm failing this", "the migration failed"), a verdict stated only inside double quotes, **the both-keyword CONTRAST lines** — a line naming both keywords reads as the neutral protocol echo, so a reviewer contrasting the example with their own call publishes the example's PASS (`Ignore the example's PASS — this is a FAIL.` / `The example says pass; my verdict is fail.`, verified at `547c6e1` and pinned executably in `tests/test_f44.py::test_review24_known_limit_both_keyword_contrast_publishes`; no keyword tweak — that re-runs rounds 2-3), and the quoted-example-verbatim case the prose scan can never structurally distinguish. False fires that cost one retry then an escalation (fail-closed, never a wrong publish): "No tests fail.", "Previously the suite would fail on 3.11", "does not pass"/"won't pass" negation mirrors. An in-band keyword scan is fighting the quoted example; three rounds of heuristics each left or opened a hole — the nonce removes the ambiguity at its source.
   - **Goal (b) is NOT met.** Three more working forges, all real-pytest-verified in this round (`test_review23` pins them EXECUTABLY — F-46 cannot land without flipping it): (A) `def pytest_pyfunc_call(pyfuncitem): return True` — tests never run, "2 passed" exit 0; (B) a `pytest_runtest_call` hookwrapper with `outcome.force_result(None)` — swallows the failure, "2 passed" exit 0; (C) `pytest_collection_modifyitems` dropping the failing test — "1 passed" exit 0 with **no** "deselected" count, invisible in the output (round 3's claim that session hooks "can at most print" is WRONG; corrected). `setattr(rep, "outcome", "passed")` also unflagged. Hook-name allowlists lose to pytest's hook surface; **F-46 M-4 owns the class** (conftest.py and the pytest config surface protected by a schema-level default a profile can only widen — a human reviews every conftest/config change), plus a proposed F-46 note: flag ANY added `def pytest_*` / `hookimpl` / `hookwrapper` / `pytest_plugins` in a conftest, with the honest F-10 sandbox conftest handled by where its content lives (a string fixture inside a test), never by exempting the filename.
   - **The junitxml claim, corrected:** the out-of-band result file (F-46 note) closes the EXIT/PRINT forgery class only — an outcome-flipping hook rewrites the report that lands in the XML too, so the file does NOT close the outcome-flip class; that class needs the F-46 M-4 protected-surface policy. The earlier round-2/3 notes saying it was "the real fix for findings 2 and 6" were half wrong: real fix for the exit class, necessary-but-insufficient for the outcome class.

**Goals, restated plainly:** (a) NOT met — met against every shape probed to date, with the in-band ambiguity class open pending the nonce work order (owner's call whether to open it); (b) NOT met — the exit/capture/JS/makereport-assignment families flag, the conftest hook family (A/B/C and the `pytest_*` surface) does not, owned by F-46 M-4. F-44's own CONTRACT (M-1, m-10, m-8, I-11) is met and green — with the amendment below.

**Contract amendment for F-04 reconcile (recorded here, not applied — `tasks.json` is the owner's; the amendment lands through reconcile or its own reviewed PR).** The queue's m-8 and m-10 texts describe the narrow pre-review versions; rounds 1-4 filled holes in both, and the reconciled criteria should read as this PR delivers and tests them:

- **m-8, amended:** `parse_test_count` reads the count from the runner's FINAL RESULT LINE (colour stripped first; any combination of passed/failed/skipped/deselected parts, or `no tests ran`; jest's `Tests:` line), never the maximum anywhere in the output — a test that prints `999999 passed` mid-run, from `atexit` after the summary, or inside the summary line does not clear the floor; a result line reporting no passed count reads 0; two result lines read unknown (one runner per test command). Evidence: `test_ac_3`, `test_review1`-`test_review4`, the hostile probes `test_hostile_fake_count_printed_mid_run`/`_after_the_summary`. The in-process print-then-hard-exit class is NOT a parser fix: the `runner_exit_forgery` sentinel covers the exit/capture/JS/outcome-assignment shapes (`test_review8`-`test_review10`, `test_review16`, `test_review17`, `test_review21`), with the conftest hook family (forges A/B/C, pinned in `test_review23`) owned by F-46 M-4.
- **m-10, amended:** `_extract_json` refuses output carrying more than one valid verdict object with differing verdicts, AND fails closed when the reviewer's real verdict object is malformed (duplicate keys, truncation, trailing comma) beside a parseable example, when a truncated verdict-bearing line follows the chosen object, and when prose contradicts the chosen object's verdict on either side (markdown emphasis, headings, bullets, apostrophe prose; an agreeing token is an echo) — one retry via the existing path, then fail-closed, the reason naming a position never content. Evidence: `test_ac_2`, `test_review11`, `test_review12`, `test_review14`, `test_review15`, `test_review18`, `test_review19`, `test_review22`; `test_f09.py::test_review6_conflicting_verdict_objects_are_invalid`. The in-band ambiguity class (the nonce work order's: both-keyword contrast lines pinned in `test_review24`, `FAILED`/`Rejected:` phrasings, homoglyphs, gerunds, quoted-only verdicts) is deliberately OUTSIDE this criterion — no keyword heuristic closes it.
