## F-45 — Pre-lease, publish and crash-window robustness

**Commit:** `feat: pre-lease, publish and crash-window robustness (F-45)` on `feat/f45-crash-window-robustness`, branched from `362203b` (F-44's merge — the WO's stated precondition; the branch was fast-forwarded to it before any work after the owner merged PR #100).

**Built (owner-directed; every file below is a protected judge/gate path — the owner directed changing them in this WO, as in F-42):**

- `factory/orchestrator.py` — the stale-PR sweep runs under `queue.idle_lease()` (AC-1/M-5); `_progress_best_effort` makes every orchestrator `ProgressLog.append` telemetry, never control flow (AC-2/M-6, AC-4/m-13); `finish()` sets `round_attempt_landed` right after `append_attempt` and the round handler skips its own append when the flag is set (AC-4/m-13); `_fetch_sha`/`_build_on_remote_default`/`_ls_remote_heads`/the sweep's local merge route through `factory.publish.remote_call`.
- `factory/publish.py` — `remote_call`, the ONE prompt-disabled, timed wrapper every remote call shares (AC-3/M-7): `GIT_NET_ENV`/`GH_NET_ENV`/`NET_TIMEOUT_SEC` live here; push, the ls-remote readback and `gh pr create` route through it; `RemoteCallTimeout` ⊂ `PublishError`.
- `factory/state.py` — `_atomic_write_json` fsyncs the parent DIRECTORY after the rename (AC-6/I-3); the run-lease holder line carries `pid=<pid>` and `_lease_busy_message`/`_pid_alive` name the holder only when the kernel says that pid is alive (AC-7/I-4).
- `factory/mergeorder.py` — `gh_pr_state` maps every rc-0 non-object/empty shape to the documented unresolvable `PublishError` (AC-5/m-14); `classify_protection` gains the same guard (amended AC-5, below).
- `tests/test_f26.py` (protected) — review pins 12/21/30/36 re-seated: they spied `orchestrator.subprocess.run`, the boundary F-45 moved behind `publish.remote_call`; each now observes `publish.subprocess.run` with the SAME assertions (LC_ALL=C env, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 + timeout on every remote call, merge/build-fetch timeouts → RunError, local-pathology wording). Pin strength unchanged; observation point moved with the seam.
- `tests/test_f45.py` (new, added to `paths.protected` in `.factory/project.yaml` beside test_f43/test_f44) and `.factory/project.yaml`.

**Design notes:**

- **AC-2 choice — best-effort append, not order-after-transition.** (a) `_log_vents` already establishes the rule: the progress log is telemetry; the queue + attempt records are the state machine. (b) Ordering the append after the transition still lets an OSError crash the run there — fixing the wedge but keeping a crash-loop that re-validates (full clone+merge+gates) per run while the disk condition persists; best-effort ends the run cleanly with `needs_human`, the AC's required terminal state. (c) The entry is a projection of state recorded elsewhere — the attempt record carries escalation+note, the findings live in the stored validator report — with ONE exception (review round 2): the F-23 `finding_keys` ride ONLY the log entry (`_run_task` seeds its cross-run carried set solely from `log.entries()`), so a dropped `finish()` append means the next run does not recognise that round's findings as carried repeats and the convergence stop fires later than it otherwise would — bounded by `max_attempts`, a budget cost, never a safety loss. Applied at ALL seven orchestrator append sites (sweep escalation, close-merged, recovery, sweep-pass, finish, publish, error handler) — one policy, no half-measures.
- **AC-3 gh coverage.** gh does not honour GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT. gh 2.83.1's own environment docs (`gh help environment`, confirmed in the binary) define `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED` = "set to any value to disable interactive prompting in the terminal" — gh calls go out with `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED=1` (+ `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` for any git credential helper gh shells out to). Empirical probe (credential-free `GH_CONFIG_DIR`): unauthenticated gh fails fast with "To get started with GitHub CLI, please run: gh auth login" — no prompt path even without the var. The provable backstop is the wrapper's timeout: a real hanging fake gh (script that sleeps) yields a timed-out `PublishError` in `test_ac_3` leg 2 — whatever gh does (auth prompt, device flow, hung API), the call cannot outlive `NET_TIMEOUT_SEC`.
- **AC-1 behavior change (intended):** a concurrent `factory run`/`loop` tick now refuses at the sweep with `RunInProgressError` (exit 2, the lease's normal "one writer task per repo" message) instead of silently racing the live run's dirs to deletion — that race was M-5. The queue never wedges: the refusal is the lease working.
- **Writers under `runs/` swept:** `create_run`/`cleanup_run` call sites are exactly the importer (inside its own `idle_lease`), the sweep (leased by this WO) and `_run_task` (inside `begin_run`). `session.py`'s `runs/probe/sdk-import` lives inside a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory`, not the factory root — not a writer under `<factory_root>/runs/`. Pinned mechanically in `test_ac_1` (call-site count + idle_lease presence + source-order pin).

**Per-AC evidence (all red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, not an import error):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: deterministic race (threads + Events freezing `create_run`, no sleeps; flock contends across fds in-process exactly as across processes): RED `reclaim_runs deleted live run dirs`; GREEN reclaim returns `[]`, the sweep's dir survives, the sweep completes (`idle`, PR reported ready). Also pins the fix in source order and the create_run call-site count.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: four injection legs. Log-append OSError: RED the task wedged `in_review` with the run crashed; GREEN `needs_human`/`stale_base` + next run proceeds. REAL corrupt hash chain (line rewritten on disk): same. `append_attempt` dies: run raises, queue reloads consistent, attempts unchanged, next run escalates cleanly. `transition` dies: attempt recorded, status unchanged, next run proceeds.
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: leg 1 real `git push` against a real accept-and-never-answer TCP server — RED the push hung (no timeout); GREEN timed-out `PublishError`. Leg 2 real hanging fake gh — timed-out `PublishError`. Leg 3 subprocess-boundary spy: push + ls-remote readback + gh all timed, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, gh also `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED=1`. Leg 4 `_fetch_sha` routes through the same wrapper object and a timed-out fetch maps to `RunError`.
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: five injection legs inside `finish()` (append_attempt, log-append OSError, log-append corrupt-chain `ProgressLogError`, `trace.run_end`, transition): RED each double-spent (`['fail','orchestrator_error']`-shapes); GREEN exactly one attempt per `finish()` call (`['fail','escalated']` / `['escalated']`+escalation `orchestrator_error` / next run proceeds).
- **AC-5** `test_ac_5`: `null`, `[]`, `"OPEN"`, `42`, `{}` missing keys, empty/whitespace stdout — RED `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'get'`; GREEN every shape → `PublishError` naming the unresolvable state; happy shapes (`OPEN`/`MERGED`) and rc≠0 unchanged. Same guard proven for `classify_protection`.
- **AC-6** `test_ac_6`: RED the parent directory was never opened/fsynced; GREEN dir-fd open+fsync observed AFTER the rename (fd-reuse handled positionally), temp-file fsync still before it, `TaskQueue.save()` covered end-to-end, and a mechanical pin that no other factory module calls `os.replace`.
- **AC-7** `test_ac_7`: live pid named; dead pid (`subprocess` reaped) → stale wording, never "held by"; garbage (`reclaim` — the repo's real stale lock content — plus no-pid and non-numeric), empty file → stale; `kill(pid,0)`→EPERM (alive, another user) named; the lease's own holder line now carries the pid.

**Spec mismatch (reported, not resolved):** `docs/remediation-plan-2026-11-05.md` §F-45 AC-7 adds "The repo's stale `run.lock` content is cleared"; `.factory/tasks.json` AC-7 does not. The main checkout's `.factory/tasks.json.run.lock` (gitignored) still holds `reclaim` — untouched, per instruction, and used as a garbage-content test input. The F-45 fix makes the leftover harmless (stale content is reported as stale), which is likely why the tasks.json AC dropped the clause — owner's call at reconcile.

**Hostile pass (what it tried, what it found):** delete a live run dir outside the lease — all `create_run`/`cleanup_run` sites audited, only leased callers; the one apparent escape (`session.py` probe) is temp-dir-local. Wedge the queue via any single sweep/finish step — full injection matrices above; `_close_merged_tasks`/recovery appends are best-effort, their transitions land first, no path leaves a half-written queue (`save()` is validate→lock→re-read→rename). Hang publish via git or gh — credential prompt (env asserted), auth prompt (gh's own switch + fast-fail verified), server-never-answers (real, tested); a hung credential HELPER is covered by the timeout backstop, the documented argument. Crash mergeorder with gh JSON — all shapes covered in both parsers; deep-nesting JSON raising `RecursionError` instead of `JSONDecodeError` is unreachable through real gh (gh's own Go parser rejects it first) — noted, not handled. Lose a rename across a crash — dir-fsync is the POSIX-correct measure; a real power-loss simulation was not performed. Name a dead/garbage holder — covered matrix.

**Findings classified (scope rule):** (1) *fixed here* — the four f26 review pins' observation seam (moved with the refactor, assertions unchanged). (2) *amended ACs* — **AC-5 amended**: m-14's crash shape also lived in `classify_protection` (`gh api`, mergeorder.py:103) — the same guard now covers both gh parsers; recorded here for F-04 reconcile. **AC-2 amended** (review round 2): the AC's text names only `_sweep_escalation`, but the shipped behaviour makes every orchestrator progress-log append best-effort — all seven sites. Amended text for reconcile: *"every orchestrator progress-log append is best-effort telemetry: a failing append — OSError or ProgressLogError — never leaves a task in `in_review` or crashes a transition; the failure is reported on stderr."* Evidence: `test_ac_2` (the sweep's four injection legs), `test_ac_4` legs A/B (`finish()`'s own append). (3) *owned elsewhere* — none of the F-46/F-47/F-53 ACs cover the mergeorder timeout gap. (4) *proposed WO* — **F-61 (proposed): timed, prompt-disabled gh read-path calls** — `gh_branch_protection` (runs pre-lease in `admission`), `gh_pr_state` (runs pre-lease in `_resolve_pr_states`, before the sweep takes the lease) and `factory approve`'s `gh_pr_state` call have no timeout: a hung gh stalls the run/approve pre-lease (Ctrl-C works, no lease held, so M-7's lease-hang does not apply), but they should route through `remote_call`'s `_run` default all the same. Not built here: outside AC-3's "publish-side" text.

**Not verified:** no power-loss simulation (AC-6 argued + call-order asserted); gh prompt behaviour verified for the unauthenticated shape and via gh's documented env var, not for an authenticated interactive TTY; the race proven in-process (two threads, separate fds — same kernel flock path), not as two OS processes; the 300s default timeout not exercised at full length (tests shrink it); no real model sessions (per WO); CI on the head SHA pending below.

**Residuals (review round 2, both probed and reproduced locally — not fixed here):** (a) the best-effort cost named in design note (c): a dropped `finish()` append loses that round's F-23 `finding_keys`, so the next run re-raises carried findings as if new until the convergence stop or the budget ends it — one round of budget, never a wrong publish. (b) A dir-fsync failure AFTER the rename inside `append_attempt`: the attempt IS on disk — exactly one, because the OSError raises from the fsync BEFORE `finish()` sets `round_attempt_landed`, so the round's error handler DOES attempt a second `append_attempt`, and that re-append is rejected by the queue's on-disk CAS check (the misleading `ConcurrentWriteError`, "changed on disk … another writer?"). The task re-queues `ready` through the fresh-instance path with the budget intact; the SAME queue instance can never write again (its `_disk_doc` desynced from the file its own rename already replaced). Diagnosability cost only; no state is lost or doubled.

**Gate:** `ruff check .` clean (no warnings); `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1034 passed** (first run caught 4 f26 review pins observing the pre-F-45 subprocess seam — re-seated as above, then green).

**Status to set at reconcile:** F-45 → `done` (owner, after merge), with the AC-5 amendment and the F-61 proposal recorded above.

## F-46 — Gate evasion hardening (L0/L1)

**Built (owner-directed; every changed judge/gate path below is protected — the owner directed changing them in this WO, the F-42/F-44/F-45 precedent):**

- `factory/l0.py` (protected) — `attribution_findings` reads each commit's message per sha (`rev-list` then `git log -1 --format=%B`, bytes decoded with U+FFFD replacement via `_commit_message`), never one combined log split on a byte the message itself can carry (AC-1/M-2); `check_diff_policy` unions the schema's default runner-config globs into the protected list and adds the structural pytest-table comparison `_runner_config_change`/`_pytest_config_at` (AC-4/M-4).
- `factory/attribution.py` (protected) — `_canonical` (confusables fold → NFKC → Cf + Cc-minus-tab/newline strip → Pd→`-`) applied to every matched text in `_match`; the finding echoes the ORIGINAL line; `generated_with_footer`'s `{0,2}` connector cap becomes `[^\n]*?` (AC-2/m-17).
- `factory/sentinels.py` (protected) — `touched_paths`, the one rename/copy path rule, shared by `matched_risk_flags` and the lint-suppression sentinel's risk-path check; comment syntax still read from the NEW path, documented (AC-3/m-9).
- `factory/l1.py` (protected) — `not_collected_evidence` (one helper, both ends): exit 5, `_NOT_FOUND` shapes, jest's "No tests found", and the ref absent from a jest-style PASS/FAIL outcome list; red end FAILs `contract_not_collected` instead of accepting a fake red (`red_reasons` records `not_collected`), green end re-routes exit-5/no-tests-ran from the misleading `contract_ambiguous` ("does not exist") to `contract_not_collected` (AC-5/M-4).
- `factory/contract.py` (protected) — `_schema_document` (cached schema doc, shared with `_validator`), `runner_config_policy` (reads `$defs.defaultRunnerConfig.const`), `apply_profile_defaults` (the ONE union route); `load_profile` returns defaults-unioned profiles.
- `schema/project.schema.json` (protected) — `$defs.defaultRunnerConfig`: `protected_globs` (`**/conftest.py`, `**/pytest.ini`) + `pytest_tables` (pyproject `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`/`[tool.pytest]`, setup.cfg `[tool:pytest]`, tox.ini `[pytest]`/`[tool:pytest]`).
- `factory/eval.py`, `factory/rehearsal.py` (protected) — the synthetic fixture profiles route through `apply_profile_defaults`.
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f46.py` added beside test_f43/f44/f45.
- `tests/test_f46.py` (new, protected from the day it exists), and three protected re-seats: `tests/test_f10.py` (AC-6 hash pin + AC-11 probe re-home), `tests/test_f38.py` (AC-1/AC-5 hash pins), `tests/test_f12.py` (importer-seat protected pin).

**Design notes:**

- **Per-commit message read (AC-1).** `rev-list` lists the shas; `_commit_message` reads each body as BYTES with `--format=%B`, decoding `utf-8/replace` — the shared `_git` decodes strictly (`text=True`) and would crash the gate on a latin-1 body (probed: a raw-byte commit message is stored and echoed as-is by git). `rev-list`/`merge-base` stay on `_git` (ASCII output); the finding text is unchanged from F-42.
- **Canonical form (AC-2).** Order: confusables fold → NFKC → Cf strip → Cc strip (tab/newline kept — `\s+` must still match tabs) → Pd fold. Line structure survives, so the canonical match position maps back to the ORIGINAL line for the echo (`_match` counts newlines). Homoglyph decision: **handled** with a hand-picked Cyrillic/Greek single-letter fold (over-match is the module's standing direction; NFKC does not cross scripts). Beyond the AC list, also probed and closed: control bytes INSIDE the trailer keyword (`Co-Authored\x1e-By`), math-bold spelling, fullwidth whole phrase, soft hyphen in "Anthro­pic".
- **Collected evidence (AC-5).** Exit 5 is the primary pytest signal; "no tests ran" / jest "No tests found" are pattern evidence; the outcome-list check is positive evidence only — it fires when the runner printed PASS/FAIL-prefixed file lines and none names the ref's path (or basename), and never when no outcome lines exist, so an alien runner's silence is not proof. `not_found` (pytest's `ERROR: not found:`) stays `contract_ambiguous` at the green end — the contract names a test that isn't there. The legitimate import-error red (exit 4, "found no collectors", `red_reason == "collection"`) is pinned green in `test_ac_5`.
- **Runner-config tables (AC-4).** Whole-file globs ride the union (load_profile AND in `check_diff_policy` itself — structural, so a hand-built profile dict cannot narrow it; one matching site, no duplicate findings). pyproject/setup.cfg/tox.ini are compared table-by-table between merge-base and head (added/removed included; both-absent is clean); unparseable at EITHER end or missing refs fails closed, like the dependency rule. Content type: the suppression-sentinel syntax lookup reads the NEW path (where the added lines live and where a linter would read them); `.py`→`.json` does not flag (a `# noqa` in JSON suppresses nothing), `.py`→`.txt` still flags (formats the map doesn't know fail safe).
- **paths.protected readers, swept:** l0 (`check_diff_policy`, unioned in-code), orchestrator :1871 (profile from `load_profile` :1034), prompts :126 (receives the orchestrator's loaded profile), session :2275 (spec list built by orchestrator/importer), importer :1481 (`load_profile` :1324/:1333/:1340), eval :675/:688/:705 (`eval_profile` routed), rehearsal `_profile` (routed), dashboard/cli/status (`load_profile`). No reader bypasses the union.

**Per-AC evidence (red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, never an import error; red run on untouched `bd85663`):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: RED `assert len(findings) == 1` → `AssertionError: []` (the `\x1e` commit sailed through the split-on-separator scan); GREEN: the finding names the offending sha12 only, `\x1f` and both-byte bodies flag, clean multi-commit and human co-author pass, latin-1 bodies flag or pass clean without a crash.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: RED `assert hit is not None` → `AssertionError: None` (U+2010 trailer unmatched); GREEN: every listed shape plus the hostile extras match, the echo carries `‐` (the original), the prose/human guards stay green.
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: RED `AssertionError: []` (rename-out-of-risk noqa unflagged while `matched_risk_flags` said `payments`); GREEN: rename-out flags, rename-in flags, clean rename quiet, copy judged new-path-only (pinned), `.py`→`.json` quiet / `.py`→`.txt` flagged (pinned choice).
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: RED `AssertionError: []` (conftest edit with a profile that doesn't list it); GREEN: structural conftest finding, load_profile union (widen-only), dep-only pyproject quiet, pytest-table/setup.cfg/tox.ini/malformed-TOML/nested-pyproject flagged, all three repo profiles + eval's synthetic load with the defaults, run_l0 fails a conftest touch.
- **AC-5** `test_ac_5`: RED `assert report.verdict == "fail", report.findings` → `AssertionError: []` (the M-4 conftest's hidden red accepted as proof); GREEN: the conftest red is `contract_not_collected` with the red exit code 5 pinned, pytest.ini `--deselect` fails at the green end, the legitimate import-error red still passes with `red_reason == "collection"`, jest's "No tests found" and absent-from-outcome-list fail through a real runner template.

**Spec mismatches (reported, not resolved):** (1) AC-5 — the remediation plan names the shapes ("exit 5, or missing from the collection list"); tasks.json says only "not collected". Built to the stricter plan wording (exit 5 + patterns + the outcome-list positive evidence). (2) AC-4 — the plan says "all three profiles validate"; tasks.json says "every profile in the repo". Covered beyond both: eval's synthetic (4th) AND the rehearsal fixture (5th) are routed and pinned. (3) NEW, mine: tasks.json/plan name pytest.ini whole-file but neither names tox.ini's `[pytest]` — classified 2 and built (the configparser comparison made it one line); amended AC below.

**Hostile pass (real tools):** control bytes `\x1e`/`\x1f`/NUL in bodies and INSIDE the trailer keyword (blocked: per-sha scan + Cc strip; `test_ac_1`, `test_review3`); CRLF and empty messages (no crash, correct verdict; `test_review2`); non-UTF-8 bodies (replacement decode, still flagged/clean correctly); a merge commit carrying the trailer (flagged, `test_review1`; verified live against this repo's own history — all 4 real trailer commits found, no false positives on the other 96). Collection hiding on real pytest 9.2: `collect_ignore`/`collect_ignore_glob`/`pytest_ignore_collect` are INERT for an explicitly-passed node id (probed live — the hook is not even called for direct args; older pytest in target projects honours them, and the observable L1 keys on — exit 5/"no tests ran" — is version-independent); `pytest_collection_modifyitems` dropping the item → exit 5 (blocked, `test_ac_5`); `-k`/`--deselect` via pytest.ini addopts → exit 5 (blocked at L1; ~~the ini itself is protected at L0~~ — true only for NESTED pytest.ini until review round 1 fixed the root forms; true for every form now); `-p plugins.hider` via addopts → exit 5 (blocked, probed); a `pytest11` entry-point plugin in pyproject → no protected finding BY DESIGN, escalates `new_dependency` (probed through `check_diff_policy` — `[project.entry-points]` is packaging surface, and a hiding plugin still produces exit 5 at L1); `pytest_plugins` in a non-conftest module → pytest refuses it outright (usage error); `--confcutdir`/parent-dir conftest injection rides pytest.ini addopts → AC-4 blocks the ini (all four dedicated names, root and nested, since round 1); mode-only change on a risk path → no added lines, nothing to flag (probed); a rename rewritten past -M's similarity threshold → add+delete, the risk flag still fires via the deletion, the suppression rides the new file (pinned `test_review5`, residual below); benign corpus replay 205 rows **0 fail**, attack corpus 24 hits (five-sentinel set, `factory sentinels replay`).

**Findings classified (scope rule):** (1) *fixed here* — control-byte split of the trailer keyword; homoglyph authors (confusables fold); tox.ini `[pytest]` (also fits 2). (2) *amended ACs, for F-04 reconcile* — **AC-4 amended**: *"Test-runner configuration is protected by a schema-level default (`schema/project.schema.json` `$defs.defaultRunnerConfig`) that a profile can only widen: `**/conftest.py` and `**/pytest.ini` whole-file, and the pytest tables/sections of `pyproject.toml` (`[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, `[tool.pytest]`), `setup.cfg` (`[tool:pytest]`) and `tox.ini` (`[pytest]`, `[tool:pytest]`) compared between merge-base and head (added/removed included; unparseable or uncomparable fails closed). Every profile in the repo — including the synthetic eval and rehearsal profiles — loads with the defaults unioned."* (tox.ini and the synthetic profiles are the additions). Evidence: `test_ac_4`, `test_review6`, `test_review7`. (3) *owned elsewhere / no build needed* — jest/vitest config files (`jest.config.*`, `package.json` `jest`, setupFiles): AC-4's default list is pytest-shaped and jest IS a supported runner — a real hole in the AC's purpose, but no jest-profile project onboards yet; proposing the jest glob set be added to `$defs.defaultRunnerConfig` at SoloAdventurer onboarding (one-line schema + this mechanism; recorded for reconcile rather than built blind). `pytest11` entry-point registration: routed to the dependency rule (probed; human merge + L1 exit-5 backstop). The sentinels' `collect_ignore` awareness (the M-4 finding's "consider"): superseded — a diff-shipped conftest can no longer pass L0 (AC-4 structural; ~~at all~~ was false for a ROOT conftest until round 1 added the root forms — every depth is covered now), and the base-conftest case is exactly what AC-5's exit-5 catches; not built. ~~`.pytest.ini`: not a pytest config source; rejected~~ — **WRONG (review round 1, finding 2): pytest 9 discovers it; now whole-file protected.** (4) *proposed WO, not built* — **VALIDATOR-SPEC.md lag (owner doc, protected, not in this WO's directed list)**: L0 §3's attribution bullet should say "each commit's message read per sha (rev-list + `%B`), any bytes"; L0 §3 should add the runner-config protected-default rule; L1 §2 should record the not-collected failure. Proposed wording recorded here for an owner docs PR.

**Residuals (probed, not closed):** (a) exit-4 collection reds from a DIFF-SHIPPED crashing conftest / `tests/__init__.py` importing a head-only module / `pytest_plugins` misuse: indistinguishable in mechanism from the legitimate red-first shape (module-level import error), so failing them closed would break red-first itself; the conftest case is closed structurally by AC-4 (the diff never passes L0), the rest are L2-scrutinised (`red_reason: collection`). (b) Rename detection threshold: a ≥50%-rewritten rename presents as add+delete; the risk flag survives (deletion event), the lint finding does not (`test_review5` pin). (c) `gates.git` strict decode (`text=True`) can still crash OTHER git-output paths on non-UTF-8 bytes (diff paths, baseline reads) — only the attribution message read is byte-safe now; the CLI-facing sibling (m-2) is F-50's. (d) The outcome-list absence check trusts a basename match, so a runner printing a DIFFERENT file with the same basename would read as present (contrived; the full-path check carries the real cases). (e) The anchored trailer patterns still allow only ≤8 leading non-word chars — unchanged F-42 design, not reopened here.

### F-46 owner review, round 1: three goal-level holes (root forms, pytest's config names, the self-hiding red) — all fixed red-first

Findings 1-6 reproduced against the round-0 tree with the reviewer's probes (`/tmp/f46probe/`) and my own re-runs before any fix; `tests/test_f46.py::test_review8..test_review14` landed red first, green after.

1. **Root runner config was unprotected (CRITICAL, AC-4).** fnmatch's `**/x` needs a slash before `x`, so the default globs covered only NESTED conftest.py/pytest.ini — a root `conftest.py` or `pytest.ini` addition passed with an empty protected list (probed; the AC-5 fixtures themselves sit at the root and L0 passed both). Fixed in the schema `const`: every whole-file name carries BOTH the root form and the `**/` form. `test_review8` (red: the four root A/M legs produced no findings). The f12 importer-seat pin re-seated to the ten-glob list.
2. **pytest 9 reads four dedicated config files, not two (CRITICAL, AC-4).** Probed: `.pytest.ini`, `pytest.toml`, `.pytest.toml` each hide a named test (`addopts=-k nothing` → exit 5) and none was protected; the round-0 ledger's ".pytest.ini: not a pytest config source, rejected" was FALSE. All four names are whole-file protected now (root + any depth). **Structural guard**: `test_review10` reads `CONFIG_LOADERS_BY_NAME` from the INSTALLED pytest (`_pytest/config.findpaths` — pytest's own single source of truth for which files it discovers) and asserts every name is covered by `protected_globs` or `pytest_tables` — the next pytest that adds a name fails this suite instead of opening a hole. `test_review9` (red: six of the eight probed paths produced no findings). Amended AC-4 text below.
3. **The red end accepted the runner's not-found line as a red proof (CRITICAL, AC-5).** A contract test hiding ITSELF — `if importlib.util.find_spec('impl_v2'): def test_ac_1(): ...` (and the try/except-ImportError twin) — makes red exit 4 with "no tests ran" + "ERROR: not found: …::test_ac_1 (no match in any of […])"; `not_collected_evidence` classified it `not_found`, the red branch acted only on `not_collected`, verdict PASS with the test never run at red. Fixed: at the RED end ANY not-collected evidence fails closed — the red phase applies head's own test file, so a test that exists at head and is "not found" at red was hidden. The green end keeps the `not_found` → `contract_ambiguous` split. The legitimate import-error red is distinct and untouched (re-probed: "found no collectors", `1 error`, NO not-found and NO no-tests-ran lines — `red_reason == "collection"`, pinned again in `test_review11`). **The whole self-hiding family probed through `run_l1` (real pytest), each with its gate:** conditional `del test_ac_1` → `contract_not_collected` (red not-found); conditional `__test__ = False` → `contract_not_collected` (no tests ran); conditional `pytestmark = skip`/`xfail` → exit 0 at red → `test_proves_nothing` (and the skip-marker sentinel flags the added line at L0); empty conditional `parametrize` → exit 0 → `test_proves_nothing`; conditional class wrapper → fails both ends (`contract_not_collected` red, `contract_ambiguous` green — the ref never matches a class-only test); `collect_ignore` in the test MODULE → inert in pytest (only conftest honours it) → the test runs and fails honestly — correctly a PASS shape. Unconditional `del` fails at green (`contract_ambiguous`). No member of the family produces a fake red any more.
4. **Unicode line separators hid line-anchored trailers (HIGH, AC-2).** `\r`, U+2028, U+2029, U+0085 before a trailer all returned None: `re.MULTILINE` splits on `\n` only, and the Cc strip GLUED the trailer onto the previous line. The canonical match now runs on BOTH forms (`_canonical_forms`): the line-split form (every `str.splitlines()` boundary → newline) and the control-stripped form (which still joins a `Co-Authored\x1e-By` keyword — the two pull opposite ways for the record separators, so both run); the echo names the ORIGINAL line of whichever form hit. `test_review12` (red: all seven separators returned None).
5. **Invisible characters outside Cf split "claude" (MEDIUM, AC-2).** Variation selector (Mn), Hangul filler U+3164/FFA0 (Lo), combining acute (Mn) each evaded. The fold now strips the Unicode **Default_Ignorable_Code_Point set** (hand-listed ranges — variation selectors, CGJ, Hangul fillers, tag planes) and **Mn/Me after NFKD** (the fold moved NFKC→NFKD so accents decompose before the mark strip). Over-matching "Cláude" is the module's standing direction. `test_review13` (red — and re-tightened after its first version passed vacuously through the vendor-address class; it now uses a neutral address so only the author-name class can fire).
6. **`_pytest_config_at` read "absent" for unreadable (MEDIUM, AC-4 fail-closed).** Absence is now PROVEN (`git_rc cat-file -e`); any other read or decode failure is an ERROR and fails the comparison closed, as the docstring always claimed. `test_review14` (red: the monkeypatched read failure returned `(surface, None)`).

**Residuals added (round 1):** (a) the unbounded `generated with` connector over-matches prose that mentions an AI name later in the line — "Generated with care; reviewed by Claude Shannon's theory" FAILs. Accepted: the over-match direction is the module's standing rule (a human reads the finding), and the AI-tool requirement is the only guard that separates footer prose from the footer. Recorded rather than narrowed. (b) U+2800 (BRAILLE PATTERN BLANK) inside "Claude" still evades the author-name class (verified with a neutral address): it is a visible-blank SYMBOL (So), in neither Cf, Mn/Me nor the Default_Ignorable set — recorded rather than chasing a curated blank-character list; the vendor-address class still catches the noreply shapes, and L2 reviews the diff.

**Amended AC-4 text for F-04 reconcile (replaces the round-0 amendment):** *"Test-runner configuration is protected by a schema-level default (`schema/project.schema.json` `$defs.defaultRunnerConfig`) that a profile can only widen: whole-file protection at the root and any depth for `conftest.py` and every dedicated pytest config name pytest itself discovers (`pytest.ini`, `.pytest.ini`, `pytest.toml`, `.pytest.toml` — pinned against the installed pytest's `CONFIG_LOADERS_BY_NAME` by test), and the pytest tables/sections of the three SHARED files — `pyproject.toml` (`[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, `[tool.pytest]`), `setup.cfg` (`[tool:pytest]`), `tox.ini` (`[pytest]`, `[tool:pytest]`) — compared between merge-base and head (added/removed included; unparseable or unprovable fails closed). Every profile in the repo — including the synthetic eval and rehearsal profiles — loads with the defaults unioned."*

**Amended AC-5 text for F-04 reconcile:** *"L1 counts a red or green run only when there is positive evidence the named contract test ran: at the RED end, ANY not-collected evidence (exit 5, 'no tests ran', jest's 'No tests found', absent from the runner's outcome list, or the runner's own not-found line — the red phase applies head's own test file, so not-found at red means hidden) is a `contract_not_collected` FAIL, never a red proof; at the green end the same evidence fails `contract_not_collected`, while the runner's not-found line stays `contract_ambiguous`. The legitimate import-error red (collected, errored, 'found no collectors', `red_reason == 'collection'`) still passes."*

**Findings 1-6 classified:** 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 are all class 1 (defects in / holes in what this WO's ACs exist for — the goal was "no evasion past L0/L1", and each was an evasion); 2 and the AC-5 red-end rule also amend the AC texts (class 2, amended texts above). The round-0 classified-section corrections are struck through inline above rather than silently rewritten — the ledger is append-only history.

**Gate (this round):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1053 passed** (1046 + 7 review tests), f12 re-seated to the widened default list. Still nothing committed, pushed or opened — awaiting review.

### F-46 owner review, round 3 (full-diff pass on 1bdf56b): two confirmed evasions, three plausibles, three cleanups — all landed

Findings 1-4 and the round-3 leg of 5 reproduced on `1bdf56b` before any fix; `test_review15..test_review19` red first (review19 is a decision pin, green by design — see 5).

1. **Precomposed confusables missed (CONFIRMED).** The confusables translate ran BEFORE NFKD, so `Claudѐ` (Cyrillic ie-with-grave U+0450) decomposed to Cyrillic е + combining grave; the mark was stripped and the un-folded Cyrillic е survived → no match. Fixed: the translate now runs AFTER NFKD and the mark strip (`_fold`). Pinned with U+0450 and precomposed Greek `Clάude` (U+03AC → α + acute → mark stripped → α folded to a) on a neutral address (`test_review15`).
2. **Outcome-list substring match (CONFIRMED).** `not_collected_evidence`'s outcome-list check accepted a bare-basename substring, so `PASS src/aa.test.js` satisfied the ref `src/a.test.js` (and a root-level ref was satisfied by any nested same-named file). Fixed: the path TOKEN is captured from each PASS/FAIL line and compared exactly, or by a `/<path>` suffix only when the ref's path contains a slash (absolute-vs-relative spellings); bare-basename matching is gone. All four legs pinned (`test_review16`).
3. **`cat-file -e` read failure as "absent" (PLAUSIBLE → fixed).** Any non-zero rc — a 124 timeout, a lazy blob fetch failure in the gates' `--filter=blob:none` clone — read as "absent", and absent-at-both-ends compares equal: fail-open. Absence is now proven from the TREE only (`git ls-tree <ref> -- ":(literal)<path>"` — no blob needed, literal pathspec verified against odd paths); every other failure (dead git, timeout, broken ref, undecodable listing) is an ERROR and fails the comparison closed. Pinned with a stubbed dead `ls-tree` (`test_review17`), plus the odd-literal-path legs (absent at main; present-but-garbage at head is an error, never absent).
4. **ConfigParser lowercased option names (PLAUSIBLE → fixed).** Probed first: capital-A `Addopts` is INERT in pytest 9.2 (test runs; lowercase deselects, exit 5) in both setup.cfg and tox.ini — so an inert `Addopts` at base flipped to a live `addopts` at head compared equal and passed. Fixed: `parser.optionxform = str`; option names compare case-sensitively. Pinned for both files, with the clean leg (inert option unchanged beside an unrelated section edit stays quiet — `test_review18`).
5. **Param-id refs fail closed (PLAUSIBLE → decided: keep, record).** Probed: a ref naming `test_ac_1[v2]` where the parametrize list grows with the impl fails `contract_not_collected` at red even though the bare function IS collected (with `[v1]`). **Kept fail-closed**: exempting `[param]` refs when the bare function collects would reopen a shaped evasion (a param list conditioned on `find_spec` hides the named case's red behind its sibling — the exemption cannot prove the red covered THE NAMED case), the factory's contract convention is exact function names, and the honest shape exists and is pinned green: name the bare function, which collects every param at red (the base legs fail) and passes at head. AC-5 amendment below. `test_review19` pins both legs executably.
6. **Cleanup** — `_match` computes `_canonical_forms` once per text before the pattern loop (was 2×N folds per message); pattern-priority order unchanged.
7. **Cleanup** — `gates.git` gains an `errors` decode parameter (default `"strict"`, every existing caller identical) and `_commit_message` passes `"replace"` through the ONE shared hardened invocation — the local subprocess duplicate is gone, so future hardening of `gates.git` reaches the attribution scan (its docstring says why the one override exists).
8. **Cleanup** — Google-style Args/Returns added to `not_collected_evidence`, `touched_paths`, `_fold`, `_canonical_forms`, `_commit_message`.

**AC-5 amendment (round 3, for F-04 reconcile; extends the round-1 text):** *"...a ref naming a parametrize id that cannot exist on the base commit (the param list itself is what the change grows) also fails `contract_not_collected` at red — deliberately: the red proof must cover the named case, and the honest contract shape is the bare function name, which collects every param at red. Pinned in `test_review19`."*

**Residuals unchanged otherwise;** the round-1 residuals (footer over-match; U+2800 braille blank) stand.

**Gate (this round):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1058 passed** (1053 + 5 round-3 tests). test_f38 untouched (the CI flake is owned on `fix/ci-xdist-flakes`).

## F-47 — Sandbox read boundary and toolkit staging

**Built (owner-directed; every changed judge/gate path below is protected — the owner directed changing them in this WO, the F-42/F-44/F-45/F-46 precedent):**

- `factory/sandbox.py` (protected) — `build_settings` denies the resolved factory root itself (AC-1/M-9), keeps the `runs/`, `holdouts`, home, `~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh` denies (defence in depth), and computes toolchain carve-outs below the factory root as well as below `$HOME` (`_toolchain_allowread(factory_root, …)`); `_refuse_unsafe_factory_root` refuses `/` and any root at/above a system read root (`_SYSTEM_READ_ROOTS`: `/usr`, `/etc`, `/private` on macOS, …) with a `SandboxError` naming why. `run_sandboxed` sets `force_closed_pipes` (the pipe names still open at the drain deadline; `()` otherwise) on the returned `CompletedProcess` AND on the raised `TimeoutExpired` (AC-2/I-9) — no trace I/O in this layer; `escapee_event` is the one event shape.
- `factory/orchestrator.py` (protected) — `sandboxed_runner(..., on_escapee=)` reports `gate:<check>` escapees on the normal and timeout paths (stderr when no sink); the attempt's gates pass `trace.append`; the stale-PR sweep passes `_sweep_trace_sink` (a `TraceWriter` for the sweep's work run id, created only on the first event).
- `factory/session.py` (protected) — `_run_prepared` (the ONE per-session step: single session, reviewer guard, fan-out, importer) calls `_report_escapee` on the normal return and before re-raising a timeout: trace sink if given; else the run's own trace file when a run id and factory root are known (the importer); else stderr. Never raises.
- `factory/trace.py` (protected) — `sandbox_escapee` joins `EVENT_TYPES` (documentation of the vocabulary; the stdout forwarder still passes `tool_call` only, so a session can neither forge nor suppress one).
- `factory/status.py` — `_week_escapees` counts top-level `sandbox_escapee` events in `trace/<project>/*.jsonl` within the week window and names their run ids; a new "Sandbox escapees this week" section; unreadable trace files are named in the notes.
- `factory/toolkit.py` (protected) — `_check_entry` judges an MCP descriptor against `vetted_allowlists(report)` — the single `- allowlists:` line `render_report` writes in the vet report the index already ties to the content hash — instead of the descriptor's own `domains` (AC-3/I-2). Missing / duplicated / variant / non-domain lines fail closed. `stage_toolkit` copies each item's index-pinned files one by one (`copy2`, `follow_symlinks=False`), creates `mcp/` explicitly, refuses two items sharing a dir and a descriptor colliding with staged content, and checks the whole staged tree is EXACTLY {plugin manifest, pinned files, one descriptor per MCP item} besides the per-item re-hash (AC-4/I-19).
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f47.py` added beside test_f43..f46.
- `tests/test_f47.py` (new, protected from the day it exists); one protected re-seat: `tests/test_f22.py::_write_reports` now writes the `- allowlists:` line for MCP entries (the descriptor's own domains — the "vetted for the project that allowed them" shape), so f22 AC-5's resolve-time per-project refusal is still the check that fires.

**Design notes:**

- **Deny and carve-outs (AC-1).** `denyRead` = home, **the factory root**, holdouts, `~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh`, `runs/`. `allowRead` (the explicit carve-outs) = the run dir, srt's package dir, the toolchain reads (now below home AND below the factory root), the caller's `allow_read` (the staged runner and staged toolkit under `<root>/sandbox/`), and the session's scratch (`scratch_write`/`writable_roots`, under `<root>/sandbox/`). Mechanism checked in the pinned srt source: on Linux each denyRead tmpfs re-binds its write paths first and then its read carve-outs (`pushReadDenyDirMounts`), shallow-first, so `<root>` → `<root>/runs` nesting restores the run the same way the existing `runs/` deny already did; on macOS Seatbelt's allow-after-deny is what the existing home deny already relies on.
- **Overlap cases, decided.** (a) Root contains `_REPO_ROOT` (srt + factory source + a repo-local venv): **works** — srt's package and the toolchain are carved out; the factory source stays denied (the session reads only its staged runner). Proven with real srt (`test_overlapping_roots_…[repo_root]`: the venv interpreter runs, `CLAUDE.md` is denied). (b) Root `= $HOME` or an ancestor of `$HOME` (`/Users`): **works** — the deny only widens over other homes; proven with real srt (`[home_parent]`). (c) Root `/` or at/above a system read root (`/usr`, `/etc`, `/private`): **refused** with a `SandboxError` naming the root and why. (d) Symlinked root: denied at its **resolved** path, explicit or derived (macOS `/tmp`→`/private/tmp`, `/var`→`/private/var`; `test_symlinked_factory_root_…`; the hostile probe ran through the unresolved `/var/folders` spelling).
- **Escapee signal (AC-2).** `force_closed_pipes: tuple[str, ...]` (`"stdout"`/`"stderr"`) on `CompletedProcess` and `TimeoutExpired`, set only when the bounded drain expired with an fd still open. Event: `{"type": "sandbox_escapee", "label": "gate:<check>" | "session:<seat>", "streams": [...], "timed_out": bool}` + the writer's stamps (seq, ts, run_id, task_id, attempt). One event name, built by `sandbox.escapee_event`.
- **Who emits.** Gate runner (attempt gates → the attempt trace; stale-PR sweep → its work run's trace, lazily; eval → stderr); `_run_prepared` (single session, reviewer, fan-out → `trace.append`; importer → its run's trace file; no run id → stderr). The SDK probe never sees the flag acted on: it runs one fixed `import claude_agent_sdk` with a throwaway temp root — recorded, not wired (nothing model-driven runs there).
- **Vetted-allowlist source (AC-3).** The vet report is the only independent record of what an item was vetted against: `factory toolkit vet` judges against the chosen profile's docs+network allowlists and `render_report` writes them as `- allowlists: a, b` (`(none)` when empty); the index already requires that report to name the item's content hash, so the record is tied to this exact content. Parsed strictly: exactly one line mentioning `allowlists:` in any bullet/indent/case form, in the exact render form, bare-domain entries only — else refuse. Matching reuses `_host_allowed` (exact or subdomain, case-sensitive — a case-only difference refuses, fail closed). The per-project re-check in `resolve_toolkit` is unchanged.
- **Staging copy method (AC-4).** File-by-file from `entry["files"]` — the same pins the pre-copy check proves equal to the source and the post-copy re-hash compares against — so "what is staged" and "what is verified" are one list. `copy2` keeps mode bits (a vendored `{staged}/…` executable stays executable, `test_staging_preserves_executable_bits`); `follow_symlinks=False` copies a post-check link swap as a link, which the re-hash refuses. Empty dirs are no longer staged (copytree staged them; nothing pins them).

**Per-AC evidence (red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, never an import error; red run on untouched `470a5ff`):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: RED `AssertionError: [gate] trace (<tmp>/factory-root/trace/fixture/20260101T000000Z-aaaaaa.jsonl) was readable inside the sandbox with the factory root outside $HOME` (output: `trace:ok`, `progress:ok`, `sessions:ok`, …). GREEN: for the gate (local binding), single-session (staged runner re-allowed) and fan-out (read-only + own scratch) shapes, real srt denies trace/progress/sessions/holdouts/another run/another project/the settings dir/a loose root file, while the own run, the own runner and the scratch write succeed; the root is in `denyRead` for every shape.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: RED `AssertionError: escapee held the pipes but no flag: None`. GREEN: real srt (macOS) flags the setsid escapee within the grace; a pin-reporting pass-through srt shim proves the drain's force-close on every platform (normal and timeout paths: `{"stdout","stderr"}`); a normal command sets `()`; through the real gate runner the escapee lands in the run's trace as `sandbox_escapee` (`label: gate:test`, run id stamped) and a clean gate command writes none; `factory status` shows `1 force-closed …` with the run id.
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: RED `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ToolkitError` (the unvetted `collector.evil.example` loaded — the tautology). GREEN: refused naming the domain; the same entry with the domain vetted loads; the real `toolkit/index.yaml` loads.
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: RED `FileNotFoundError: … /staged/mcp/lookup.json` (descriptor write relied on an earlier copytree creating `mcp/`). GREEN: an MCP item vendored under `plugins/` stages its descriptor at `mcp/lookup.json`; the staged file set EQUALS {manifest, pinned files, descriptor}; an unpinned source file refuses and leaves no staged tree.
- Edge tests (red-first where they pin new behaviour): root refusal (`/`, `/usr`, `/etc`, `/private`); overlap roots with real srt (repo root, `$HOME`'s parent); symlinked root; report allowlists fail-closed (missing, spoof duplicate, case-only, subdomain admitted); session `_run_prepared` emits on return and on timeout; status per project in the week window (old/other-project/nested-forged/malformed lines ignored); shared item dir refused; exec bit kept; whole-tree equality catches a stray file; `run_once` hands the gate runner the run trace (escapee lands in THAT run's trace with its task id, and in status).
- **Mutation checks** (each reverted after): dropping `on_escapee=trace.append` → wiring test red; dropping the session's normal-path report → red; dropping the timeout flag → `test_ac_2` red; restoring the load tautology → `test_ac_3` red; disabling the whole-tree equality → `test_staged_tree_equality…` red.

**Spec notes (reported, not resolved):** (1) AC-1 names carve-outs for "the run's own dir and srt"; the code also needs (and keeps) the toolchain reads, the caller's `allow_read` (staged runner + staged toolkit — the session's own) and the scratch dirs — each is the session's own state or the interpreter it runs on; none re-opens a sibling store (proven by the AC-1 markers, incl. the settings dir that holds other sessions' runners). (2) AC-3's "vetted allowlist" is undefined in the contract; read as the `- allowlists:` line of the item's vet report (the record the index already ties to the content hash) — see design notes. (3) AC-2 "counts in factory status": built **per project, over the status page's 7-day window**, with the run ids listed (a count without the runs gives the owner nothing to rotate against).

**Hostile pass (real srt on macOS, factory root outside `$HOME`, reached through the unresolved `/var/folders` symlink spelling, with a space in the name and again with `fäctory-röot ✓`):** each route run on this tree AND on a baseline with only the factory-root deny removed, so "blocked" is proven to be this WO's doing. Python `open` of trace — baseline LEAK, F-47 denied (EPERM). `cat` — LEAK → denied. Relative `../../../trace` walk from cwd — LEAK → denied. Symlink planted in the run dir → `../../../trace` — LEAK → denied (Seatbelt checks the resolved path). node `fs.readFileSync` from the toolchain — LEAK → `EPERM`. `os.listdir(root)` — listed `trace, sandbox, runs` → denied. Hard link from the trace into the run — denied at both (Seatbelt refuses the link). Git `alternates` in the run's `.git/objects/info` pointing at another run's objects + `cat-file` of a blob only that run has — blocked at both (the `runs/` deny; the object dir is unreadable). `git -C <other run>` / `git --git-dir` — blocked at both. `/proc/self/root` — n/a on macOS (ENOENT); Linux not run. Root equal to the repo root and an ancestor of `$HOME`: real-srt tests (interpreter runs, repo file denied). Escapee shapes (real srt): double-fork after setsid → flagged `('stdout','stderr')`, survives; closes stderr only → flagged `('stdout',)`; no-setsid background child → killed by killpg, no flag (correct); **setsid + close stdout AND stderr → no flag, survives** (finding (4) below). stdin is never a pipe of ours (inherited), so holding it is irrelevant to the drain. Index: subdomain of a vetted domain admitted (the resolve rule); case-only difference refused; allowlists line missing / duplicated / spoofed into the body / indented variant refused. Staging: pinned dotfile staged; empty dir NOT staged (copytree used to); file added after resolve refused, nothing left; pinned file swapped for a symlink refused.

**Findings classified (scope rule):**
- (1) *fixed here* — two items sharing one vendored dir (copytree crashed with FileExistsError; a file-by-file copy would have merged them silently) → explicit refusal; a descriptor colliding with staged content → refusal; the whole-tree equality; exec bits kept by the new copy; a factory root at/above a system read root → `SandboxError`; toolchain carve-outs under the factory root (else a root containing the checkout would hide its venv).
- (2) *amended ACs, for F-04 reconcile* — **AC-1 amended**: *"`denyRead` covers the (resolved) factory root itself, with explicit carve-outs for the run's own dir, srt's package, the toolchain, the caller's staged reads and the session's own scratch; a root whose deny would hide the system (`/`, or at/above `/usr`, `/etc`, …) is refused. A real-srt test with `--factory-root` outside `$HOME` asserts a session cannot read `trace/`, `progress/`, `sessions/`, `holdouts/`, another run, another project or the settings dir, for the gate, single-session and fan-out settings."* **AC-2 amended**: *"A drain that force-closes a `setsid` escapee sets `force_closed_pipes` on the CompletedProcess/TimeoutExpired; every caller that owns a trace (gate runner, session seats, stale-PR sweep, importer) writes one `sandbox_escapee` event; `factory status` counts them per project for the week, naming the runs."*
- (3) *owned elsewhere* — Linux behaviour of the escapee (bwrap's pid namespace kills it with srt, so the alarm should never fire there) and the `/proc/self/root` route: F-58 (Linux sandbox host) — `test_ac_2`'s real-srt leg asserts flag-consistency on Linux rather than a fixed outcome; fixed-sleep timing in the new escapee tests uses a 2 s grace with ≥5 s margins (the F-51 hygiene class); the SDK probe's throwaway temp root does not deny the real root — nothing model-driven runs there (one fixed import), recorded not wired; wheel installs resolving schema/toolkit via `parent.parent` — INFO, not this WO.
- (4) *proposed WO, not built* — **the silent escapee**: a process that `setsid`s and closes both stdout and stderr leaves no pipe open, so no drain signal exists; on macOS it survives holding the seat token (probed). The drain cannot see it by construction. Proposal: a post-run process sweep on macOS (same-uid processes that are sandboxed per `sandbox_check` and whose sandbox denies the run's sibling paths, or a per-run marker), alarming through the same `sandbox_escapee` event — plus the owner-action question of auto-rotating the seat token on any escapee (today: alarm only; rotation stays an owner action). Other reachable dirs reviewed: the target repo's `.factory` is inside the session's own clone (by design); `node_modules` beyond srt's package stays denied (only the package dir is carved out); toolchain carve-outs are `bin/` + sibling `lib/` + venv site-packages (global npm packages under an nvm `lib/` are readable — no credentials live there by convention, `.npmrc` is in the denied home); writable scratch outside the factory root — none found (reviewer and fan-out scratch live under `<root>/sandbox/`).

**Residuals (probed, not closed):** the silent escapee above; a factory root like `/opt` that holds a Homebrew toolchain is not refused — the carve-outs cover `bin/`+`lib/` only, so such a setup would fail loudly at exec (never a weakened deny); the descriptor-domain rule is `_host_allowed` (subdomains admitted), which is broader than srt's exact-host network allowlist — declarative only, egress is still enforced by srt with the profile's lists.

**Not verified:** this session ran the real-srt tests on **macOS only** (Seatbelt). The Linux runs were done **by the reviewer** in an ubuntu:24.04 container with real srt + bwrap (round 1, on the pre-fix tree): 2 failed / 1070 passed — the two failures are the round-1 findings below; the reviewer's Linux probes confirmed `trace/` under a root outside `$HOME` is unreadable (FileNotFoundError), fan-out scratch writes and single-session run-dir writes persist on the host, read-only run-dir writes are denied. The fixed tree's Linux re-run is the reviewer's (pending at the time of writing); the escapee being killed by bwrap's pid namespace is still reasoned, not observed. No real model session ran (per WO); the session/fan-out escapee path is proven with a stubbed `run_sandboxed`, the gate path with real srt and a shim.

### F-47 owner review, round 1: two Linux-only findings — both fixed

Found by the reviewer's ubuntu:24.04 container run (real srt + bwrap): 2 failed, 1070 passed; `test_f05::test_ac_1` passes on main in the same container, so finding 1 is this WO's regression.

- **Finding 1 (class 1, CI-blocking): `tests/test_f05.py::test_ac_1` — `outside_run:ok` on Linux.** Cause (reviewer-proven): srt on Linux implements a read-deny on a DIRECTORY as a tmpfs mounted over it; with the factory root now denied, the write to `<root>/probe-outside.txt` lands in that tmpfs and never reaches the host (`exists()` False afterwards). Adding the root to `denyWrite` changes nothing (reviewer-tested). **Re-seat (protected test, same or greater strength, on the real invariant — the host):** both platforms assert the outside-run and /tmp files do not exist on the host afterwards, and now also that the inside write DID reach the host; macOS keeps the in-sandbox `denied` assertion (Seatbelt really refuses); Linux accepts `ok` ONLY together with host absence; `outside_tmp` stays `denied` on both. The semantics are documented in the `sandbox.py` module docstring and beside the `denyRead` list in `build_settings`. Sweep for other in-sandbox write-refusal assertions under the factory root: only `test_f30.py::_sandbox_write_refused` (the trace path) — on Linux that write hits an empty tmpfs where `trace/fixture/` does not exist, so it still fails, and the test already asserts host absence; every other write-refusal test targets a path outside the root (`/tmp`, f10 AC-11's `tmp_path/outside`, f43's `/tmp` probe) or the run's `.git/hooks`/`config` / a read-only run dir, which the root tmpfs does not change (consistent with the reviewer's run: nothing else failed).
- **Finding 2 (class 1, test defect): `test_overlapping_roots_…[home_parent]` — `inside_root:ok` on Linux.** Cause: the probe (`REPO_ROOT/CLAUDE.md`) is inside `Path.home().parent` only when the checkout lives under it (the container's was at `/w47`) — a layout accident, not a sandbox hole. Fix: `$HOME` faked to `tmp/home/me`, the root is `tmp/home`, the probe a SIBLING home `tmp/home/other/secret.txt`. **A second defect found while proving the fix by mutation:** with the root deny removed the leg still passed on macOS, because the run dir `tmp/run` made the derived `runs/` deny (`run_dir.parent.parent`) the pytest base dir, which covered the probe. The run dir now lives in a canonical layout away from the root (`tmp/elsewhere/runs/<project>/<id>`), and the test asserts no other `denyRead` entry covers the probe; mutation re-checked: root deny removed → `inside_root:ok` → red. `[repo_root]` is unchanged (its probe is inside the root by construction).

**Gate (final tree, macOS):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1073 passed** (1058 pre-existing + 15 F-47; same count as round 0 — the round-1 fixes changed tests, added none), 12 warnings (pre-existing `re.split` maxsplit deprecation in toolkit.py). Protected re-seats: `tests/test_f22.py::_write_reports` (same strength — f22 AC-5's resolve-time refusal still fires) and `tests/test_f05.py::test_ac_1` (round 1, host invariant — stronger on both platforms); no assertion weakened.

**Status to set at reconcile:** F-47 → `done` (owner, after merge), with the AC-1/AC-2 amendments and the silent-escapee WO proposal recorded above.

## F-48 — Eval parity and variance

**Built (owner-directed; every protected change below was directed in this WO's brief, the F-46/F-47 precedent):**

- `factory/l2.py` (protected, owner-directed) — NEW public `settle_review_verdict(union, prior, delta_files, *, delta_mode, vote=None, reviewers=1, final=False)`: the post-review verdict chain as ONE function — union → optional verifier vote (`apply_vote`) → `apply_convergence` (with `delta_files=None` normalised to `frozenset()`, exactly the orchestrator's first-round call) → `final_review_verdict` when `final=True`. Module docstring gains the F-48 paragraph. `apply_convergence`/`apply_vote`/`final_review_verdict` themselves are untouched.
- `factory/orchestrator.py` (protected, owner-directed) — both L2 verdict sites render through the shared function: the per-round review now calls `settle_review_verdict(union, prior, delta_files, delta_mode=delta is not None, vote=vote, reviewers=ctx.config.reviewers)` (M-3 comment at the site); the final review the same with `frozenset()`, `delta_mode=False`, `final=True`. The verifier still only runs when `union.parse_error is None and union.findings`; the verifier-budget aborts and `verifier.status != "completed"` notes are unchanged at the sites; the two now-dead `l2 = union` assignments were removed. Imports: the four function imports replaced by `settle_review_verdict` (plus the unchanged `merge_reviews`/`parse_*`).
- `factory/eval.py` (NOT protected — see finding (4a)) —
  - `run_l2_review` (AC-1): the verdict is settled by `settle_review_verdict(union, {}, frozenset(), delta_mode=False, vote=vote, reviewers=reviewers)` — first-round mode, no final review (the mode argument is recorded in the spec notes and asserted in `test_ac_1`'s orchestrator leg).
  - `run_l2_review` (AC-2): quota is checked on the fan-out's first-run records BEFORE any parse or re-run (`return None, 0` — no tokens burned, no retry counted); post-parse, quota on a re-run record still voids (`None, retries` — a spent re-run is counted, tokens were burned); a quota-hit VERIFIER voids before its output is parsed as a vote (the same production order — previously the dead session parsed as a no-vote that kept every finding).
  - `run_seeded_eval` (AC-4): `repeats: int = 1` param (bools and <1 refused with `EvalError`); each seed/clean diff runs N times, every record carrying its 1-based `repeat`; report gains `repeats`, `catch_rate_by_repeat` (per-repeat rate, `None` per unjudged repeat), `catch_rate_stats` (mean + sample stdev + min–max + `repeats_judged`; `stdev: None` at one value), `false_fail_on_clean` (per-layer `{failed, judged}` or `None`, replacing the L2-only `l2_false_fail_on_clean` integer), `false_fail_by_repeat`/`false_fail_stats` (per-repeat fail COUNTS). Quota-voided runs leave every denominator, counted on `l2_quota_voided` — as before.
  - `render_report` (AC-3/AC-4): the clean per-diff line names L0, L1 AND L2, a failed layer marked `(false positive)` (an invalid-verdict-json fail reads `L2 fail (false positive, invalid_verdict_json)`); quota-voided L2 still reads "no verdict"; per-diff lines carry `[rN]` markers when repeats > 1; the header names the repeat count; catch-rate lines carry mean/stdev/min–max (or "spread n/a, N judged repeat(s)" — never a fake 0); a NEW "## False-fail on clean diffs" section renders per layer; the quota line names its denominator ("N of M L2 runs") when L2 ran. Old-shape JSON (no `repeats`, no `repeat`, the old aggregate key, or trimmed records) renders with fallbacks — pooled lines without a spread claim — never a crash (pinned).
  - `_clean_review_summary` rewritten (all three layers); `_clean_false_fail` replaced by `_false_fail_by_layer`; new helpers `_repeat_of`/`_per_repeat`/`_catch_rate_by_repeat`/`_false_fail_by_repeat`/`_stats`/`_repeat_tag`.
  - `run_on_demand_eval` gains `repeats: int = DEFAULT_REPEATS` (=3) passed through to the runner; `DEFAULT_REPEATS = 3` documented as the contract's number pending owner confirmation.
- `factory/cli.py` — `factory eval --repeats N` (`type=int`), validated in `_run_eval` (`< 1` → exit 2 with a message, before any config load; argparse refuses non-integers with its own exit 2), default `DEFAULT_REPEATS`, passed to `run_on_demand_eval`.
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f48.py` added beside test_f43..f47.
- `tests/test_f48.py` (new, protected from the day it exists): `test_ac_1`..`test_ac_4` + 8 review/edge tests.
- Re-seats (same strength, no assertion weakened):
  - `tests/test_f16.py` (protected, mechanical): the two monkeypatched `fake_run` signatures gain the `repeats` kwarg; the two on-demand driver tests pin `repeats=1` (their subject is report/cleanup plumbing, not the now-defaulted spread).
  - `tests/test_l2_review_fanout.py` (not protected — the PR #90 round-2/4 pins): six assertions re-seated from the old report shape to the new one — `(0 of 5 seeds)` → `(pooled 0 of 5 seed runs`, `L2 false-fail on clean diffs: X of Y` → `L2: X of Y clean runs` (n/a → `- L2: n/a (0 judged)`), `l2_false_fail_on_clean` integer → `false_fail_on_clean["L2"] == {failed, judged}`. The counts asserted are the same numbers.
  - `tests/test_l2_blank_missed_earlier.py` (not protected — the F-23 blank-`missed_earlier` suite's four clean-line pins, found by the first full-gate run): the fixtures now carry the real `run_seed` record shape (L0/L1 layer dicts — a real record always has them) and assert the exact new lines — `L0 pass, L1 pass, L2 pass, 0 blocking finding(s)`; the invalid-verdict-json fail reads `L2 fail (false positive, invalid_verdict_json), …`; a plain fail reads `L2 fail (false positive), 1 blocking finding(s)`; L2-skipped reads `L2 n/a (skipped)` with L0/L1 still named. Exact-line pins, stronger than before (all three layers pinned, not one).

**Design notes:**

- **The shared chain's shape (AC-1).** Production's two sites interleave session-running, budget aborts and logging around a PURE verdict tail: `apply_vote` (when a verifier ran) → `apply_convergence` (always) → `final_review_verdict` (final review only). That tail — and nothing else — is `settle_review_verdict`; the union comes in, the settled report goes out. Running seats stays at the call sites (those differ by caller by design); the arithmetic that turns reviews plus vote into a verdict cannot drift again. Behaviour byte-identical: `vote=None` reproduces the no-verifier branch exactly (the union passes straight to convergence), and `final=True` reproduces `final_review_verdict(apply_convergence(...))`.
- **Which production mode a single-round eval review is.** First-round per-round review: `prior={}` (the eval has no progress log; a fresh task's first round starts empty the same way), `delta_files=frozenset()`, `delta_mode=False` (no earlier review in the run), `final=False` — for a one-round task the per-round review IS what gates publish: the F-41 final review runs only when `final_pending` or a DELTA round passed (`reviewed and l2.verdict == "pass" and delta is not None`), and a task passing on its first attempt has `delta is None` and `final_pending=False`, so production never runs a final review for it. `test_ac_1`'s orchestrator leg drives exactly that one-round pass and asserts `delta_mode=False`, `final=False`, one call.
- **Quota order (AC-2).** Production's order, mirrored seat for seat: fan-out returns → `_is_quota` over the records → (abort) → parse with the retry policy (stop on a quota re-run) → verifier → `_is_quota(verifier)` → (abort) → vote parse → settle. Reusing `orchestrator._is_quota` (no second quota test) as directed — one more private the F-54 seam must eventually promote (finding 3a).
- **The M-3 symptom needed a non-unanimous union.** A declared-fail review whose findings the verifier vetoes flips to PASS only when the drop is ALLOWED — the unanimous rule refuses a veto of a finding every reviewer reported. Two identical scripted reviewers (the F-16 default) make every finding unanimous, so the first draft of the veto test stayed red for the WRONG reason (the veto correctly refused); the re-seated scenario splits the reviewers (one declares fail with the finding, one passes) exactly as production's own pin (test_f38's veto round) does.
- **Repeats aggregation (AC-4).** Repeat at the seed level inside `run_seeded_eval` (one runner, one report shape): repeat r runs every seed then every clean diff; each record carries `repeat`. Per-layer per-repeat catch RATE = catches/judged within that repeat; per-repeat false-fail COUNT (not rate — the owner reads "1 of 2", and a quota-shrunk denominator must not read as a smaller count). Spread = sample stdev (`statistics.stdev`) + min–max, over the repeats THAT JUDGED (a fully-voided repeat contributes no value, and the line says "N judged repeats"); one judged repeat → "spread n/a", never 0; a real zero at N≥2 renders as 0 (L0's, deterministically). Pooled counts (`X of Y seed runs`) stay beside the mean so the F-16 reading survives. `N=1` remains the library default for `run_seeded_eval` (programmatic callers and the F-16 tests keep today's cost/semantics); `DEFAULT_REPEATS=3` applies at `run_on_demand_eval`/`factory eval`.
- **Per-layer false-fail (AC-3).** A clean diff that failed L0 renders `L0 fail (false positive)` in the per-diff line AND counts in `false_fail_on_clean["L0"]` — the metric became per-layer (all three), quota-voided L2 still "no verdict" and out of the denominator.

**Per-AC evidence (red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, never an import error or typo; red run on untouched `b2efa5a`):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: RED `AttributeError: module 'factory.l2' has no attribute 'settle_review_verdict'`. GREEN: one function object bound identically in `factory.l2`, `factory.orchestrator` and `factory.eval`; a real `run_once` round (test_f38 harness, clean one-round pass → `pr_opened`) calls the spied symbol exactly once with `delta_mode=False`/`final=False`/`vote=None`; a real `run_seed` review calls the eval module's binding once with `delta_mode=False`, `vote` set, `reviewers=2`. The M-3 symptom: `test_review_declared_fail_vetoed_to_pass_in_eval` RED `AssertionError: {'layer': 'L2', 'verdict': 'fail', ...} — assert 'fail' == 'pass'` → GREEN the vetoed declared fail renders PASS with the veto on `verifier_dropped` and no catch booked.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: RED `AssertionError: {'quota_voided': True, 'review_retries': 1} — assert 1 == 0` (the quota seat was parsed, re-run and counted). GREEN: `{"quota_voided": True, "review_retries": 0}`, exactly 2 reviewer calls (the fan-out, no re-run), no verifier call. The verifier half: `test_review_verifier_quota_voids_the_review` RED (a full L2 report returned where the voided shape is owed) → GREEN `{"quota_voided": True, "review_retries": 0}`. No-regression pin: `test_review_parse_failed_seat_keeps_its_one_rerun` (green-on-arrival, deliberately — the retry policy predates this WO and must survive it).
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: RED `AssertionError: assert ('L0 fail' in 'L2 pass, 0 blocking finding(s)')`. GREEN: the failing-test-runner clean diff renders `L0 fail (false positive), L1 pass, L2 pass, …`; the all-pass line names all three with `0 blocking`; the quota-voided line keeps "no verdict" with L0/L1 still named; `false_fail_on_clean` reads `L0 {failed: 2, judged: 2}`, L1/L2 `{failed: 0, judged: 2}`; the render carries the per-layer section.
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: RED `TypeError: run_seeded_eval() got an unexpected keyword argument 'repeats'` (the missing surface — the F-38 red precedent). GREEN: the flaky reviewer catches the mock seed in repeat 1 and misses in repeat 2 → `catch_rate_by_repeat["L2"] == [1/6, 0.0]`, mean 1/12, stdev `statistics.stdev([1/6, 0.0])`, min 0 max 1/6; L0's real zero spread renders 0; the render says "2 repeats"; at N=1 every layer's `stdev` is `None` and the render says "spread n/a".

**Spec notes (reported, not resolved):**

1. **AC-1 mode:** first-round per-round review, `final=False` — argued in the design notes from the publish gating (`final_pending or (reviewed and pass and delta is not None)`); a one-round task never takes the final-review branch.
2. **AC-4 default and cadence need owner confirmation.** Default N=3 built as the contract states. Cost, measured from the last baseline run (`~/factory/eval/seeded-eval-20261104T222828Z.json`: every one of the 8 diffs drew findings, so 16 reviewer + 8 verifier sessions): **24 real sessions per repeat at the worst case — 72 at N=3 versus 24 at the old N=1, a 3× quota cost per eval run** (17–24 per repeat in general: only finding-drawing diffs pay a verifier). The monthly cadence is unchanged anywhere.
3. **AC-3:** the false-fail metric became per-layer (all three), counts pooled plus per-repeat spread; the catch-rate spread is over per-repeat RATES, the false-fail spread over per-repeat COUNTS.
4. **AC-5:** pending owner run — the exact command and what the report will contain are at the bottom of this entry.

**Hostile pass (real code paths, stub seats; every probe run on this tree):**

- **Line-by-line chain diff, eval vs production:** reviewer count — config-driven in both (`factory eval` reads the same `load_config` the orchestrator does; `--reviewers` overrides) ✓; prompts — both call `orchestrator._review_prompt(task, diff, l0, l1)` / `_verifier_prompt(task, diff, union.findings)` with round-1 arguments (no delta, no carried) ✓; protected list — same profile `paths.protected` ✓; system prompts — the same `assemble_reviewer_prompt`/`assemble_verifier_prompt(profile)` ✓; l0/l1 to reviewers — the same report dicts ✓; verifier skip on no-findings/parse-error ✓; parse-retry policy — the same `parse_reviews_with_retry` with `stop=_is_quota` (the eval has no attempt budget to fit, documented in `_rerun_review_seat`) ✓; quota at every seat — now production's order ✓ (this WO); verdict chain — the shared function, first-round mode ✓ (this WO). **Divergences that remain, classified below:** seat budgets (`SEAT_MAX_TURNS=20`/`SEAT_TIMEOUT_MIN=10.0` fixed vs production's per-attempt share/remaining wall — finding 4b), no `toolkit=`/`srt_bin_path`/`run_id`/`trace_sink` on the eval's seats (the fixture profile declares no toolkit, so nothing is withheld in practice; the eval's seats are untraced — finding 4b).
- **Make a metric move without the pipeline changing:** duplicate seeds — impossible from the report's inputs (the seed table is a module constant; uniqueness pinned by `test_review_seed_ids_stay_unique`; the residual is that `eval.py` is not protected — finding 4a); a seed whose diff fails to apply — probed: `EvalError: seed …: edit target not found` propagates, CLI exit 2, no silent metric; an L1/L0 crash — GateError propagates to the same exit-2 refusal (existing behaviour); a severity case variant ("Blocking") — canonical at parse since F-44 and `_count_blocking` case-insensitive (verified in the tree); a garbage verifier — probed: invalid vote drops nothing, every finding stands, the catch is kept (fail-closed preserved).
- **Quota-on-the-RETRY** (not first run): probed and pinned (`test_review_quota_on_the_rerun_still_voids`) — `{"quota_voided": True, "review_retries": 1}`: the spent re-run is counted, the review still voided.
- **Repeats where every run is quota-voided:** pinned — L2 stats `None`, per-repeat `[None, None]`, render `- L2: n/a (0 judged)`, never `L2: 0%`; the quota line reads "16 of 16 L2 runs".
- **A report rendered from pre-F-48 JSON:** pinned — old keys only, no `repeats`/`repeat` → renders as a 1-repeat report with "spread n/a"; trimmed/hand-built records (a pooled rate with no judgeable runs) fall back to the pooled line with NO spread claim rather than asserting or crashing (this fallback itself was a finding during implementation — the first draft asserted).
- **End-to-end driver with repeats=2** (real unconfined gates, scripted seats): probed — 12 seed records + 4 clean, `repeat` 1/2 correctly grouped, rendered report coherent, files written.

**Findings classified (scope rule):**

- **(1) fixed here** — the verifier-quota parse (a quota-hit verifier read as a no-vote that kept every finding: same class as m-12, fixed and pinned `test_review_verifier_quota_voids_the_review`); the render-time assert on hand-built/trimmed reports (crashed `render_report` on inputs the function has always accepted — replaced by the pooled fallback); the two dead `l2 = union` assignments.
- **(2) amended ACs** — none. Every AC was achievable and achieved as written; the verifier-quota fix is a class-1 defect fix inside AC-2's subject, not an AC hole; the mode reading and per-layer false-fail are spec notes 1 and 3.
- **(3) owned elsewhere** — (3a) `eval.py` importing `orchestrator._*` privates: F-54 AC-2 owns the seam; this WO added one more use (`_is_quota`, reuse mandated) — F-54's promotion list grows by it. (3b) `_run_eval`'s `rendered_path` OSError: F-50 AC-6, untouched here. (3c) `_run_task`'s size: F-53 owns the split; the two verdict sites got marginally simpler (one call each), which the split must carry. (3d) F-44's `_count_blocking` case-insensitivity — verified landed, nothing owed.
- **(4) proposed WOs, not built** — (4a) **protect the judge-measurer:** `factory/eval.py` is not in `paths.protected` while it scores the judges — a session could edit the seed table, the catch attribution or the false-fail counting its own validators are measured by. Proposal: an owner PR adds `factory/eval.py` to `paths.protected` (one line + a `test_profile_review` expectation), or moves the seed table/attribution into a protected module. (4b) **eval seat parity:** the eval's seats run under fixed budgets (20 turns / 10 min) instead of production's per-attempt shares, and carry no toolkit/trace wiring; a session that would exhaust a production share may complete in the eval. Proposal: derive the eval's seat caps from a documented task-budget shape (or record the divergence as accepted — the fixture's diffs are tiny and its profile declares no toolkit). (4c) **delta-mode measurement:** the eval never exercises `delta_mode=True`, the final review, or the convergence carries — a multi-round seed scenario (seed → scripted fix → delta review) would measure the F-23/F-41 machinery the same way the single-round seeds measure the first round.

**Residuals (probed, not closed):** the seat-budget divergence (4b) means L2 metrics can still differ from a production run through cap differences alone; the parity is first-round-only by design (delta and final-review modes unmeasured, 4c); L0/L1 spread is structurally 0 (deterministic gates) — the repeats' information concentrates in L2, as intended.

**Not verified:** no real model session ran (AC-5 is the owner's paid run — the parity and quota order are proven with stub seats only; the real `run_session_sandboxed` path is exercised only by the existing plumbing suites). Not run on Linux: the local gate is macOS, and F-48 touches no sandbox code — CI (ubuntu-24.04) is the first Linux run of this tree.

**Gate (final tree, macOS, frozen after the last code edit):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1087 passed** (1073 pre-existing + 14 F-48), 0 failed, exit 0. (The first full-gate run caught the four `test_l2_blank_missed_earlier` clean-line pins — re-seated above; two intermediate runs are not evidence, the final one is.)

**AC-5: pending owner run.** After merge, run:

```
factory eval --repeats 3
```

(optionally `--factory-root <root>`; `--reviewers` defaults to the factory config's, as before). The report lands at `<factory-root>/eval/seeded-eval-<stamp>.json` with the rendered `.md` beside it, and will contain: per-diff lines `[r1]..[r3]` for every seed and clean diff (clean lines naming L0/L1/L2, false positives marked per layer); catch rate per layer as the mean of 3 repeats with stdev and min–max plus the pooled counts; the per-layer false-fail section with per-repeat spread; `L2 quota-voided: N of 24 L2 runs`; reviewer retries in the header. That rendered file is the tick evidence AC-5 asks to record (the F-16 baseline was measured without convergence, single-run).

### F-48 review round 1: two findings — both fixed
